+Ordinary landings use `node snapback4/scripts/affected.mjs --base origin/main --run`
+from the repository root; add `--worktree` for staged, unstaged and untracked
+changes. `--json` prints the plan. Every command has a timeout, failures are
+collected, and receipts live in `target/affected/`. Cargo runs with
+`CARGO_INCREMENTAL=0 CARGO_PROFILE_DEV_DEBUG=0 CARGO_PROFILE_TEST_DEBUG=0`.
+The selector reads Cargo's workspace graph, closes source changes over reverse
+dependencies, narrows test-only changes to targets/files, and follows client
+imports/process consumers. Unknown paths, locks/manifests, build scripts,
+proc-macros and shared test support fail broad with a reason per file. The
+complete battery belongs to the schedule; packed qualification belongs to
+publication. An affected receipt does not qualify a release. See
+[LLP 0189](../llp/0189-verification-cadence-and-claim-gating.decision.md#snapback-4-affected-selection-2026-10-24).
+
 Crates: `snapback4-core` (value model, schema with `sync`, IR, key encoding,
 SQLite store, rules with hoisting, the meter, the interpreter, the partition
 log, at-most-once, the day-two classifier, wire forms), `snapback4-lang`

+Ordinary landings use `node snapback4/scripts/affected.mjs --base origin/main --run`
+from the repository root; add `--worktree` for staged, unstaged and untracked
+changes. `--json` prints the plan. Every command has a timeout, failures are
+collected, and receipts live in `target/affected/`. Cargo runs with
+`CARGO_INCREMENTAL=0 CARGO_PROFILE_DEV_DEBUG=0 CARGO_PROFILE_TEST_DEBUG=0`.
+The selector reads Cargo's workspace graph, closes source changes over reverse
+dependencies, narrows test-only changes to targets/files, and follows client
+imports/process consumers. Unknown paths, locks/manifests, build scripts,
+proc-macros and shared test support fail broad with a reason per file. The
+complete battery belongs to the schedule; packed qualification belongs to
+publication. An affected receipt does not qualify a release. See
+[LLP 0189](../llp/0189-verification-cadence-and-claim-gating.decision.md#snapback-4-affected-selection-2026-10-24).

     2	// @ref LLP 0189#snapback-4-affected-selection-2026-10-24 — conservative landing selection;

    11	export const ROOT = resolve(import.meta.dirname, '../..');

    18	function command(root, executable, args, options = {}) {
    19	  return execFileSync('timeout', ['120s', executable, ...args], {
    20	    cwd: root, env: { ...process.env, ...CARGO_ENV }, encoding: 'utf8', maxBuffer: 128 * 1024 * 1024, ...options,
    21	  });
    22	}
    23	function git(root, ...args) { return command(root, 'git', args); }
    24	function paths(output) { return output.split('\0').filter(Boolean); }
    25	function walk(root, path = '') {
    26	  if (!existsSync(join(root, path))) return [];
    27	  return readdirSync(join(root, path), { withFileTypes: true }).flatMap(entry => {
    28	    if (['node_modules', 'target', '.git', 'dist'].includes(entry.name)) return [];
    29	    const child = slash(join(path, entry.name));
    30	    return entry.isDirectory() ? walk(root, child) : entry.isFile() ? [child] : [];
    31	  });
    32	}

    34	// Cargo, including optional and dev dependencies, owns the workspace graph.
    35	export function loadModel(root = ROOT) {
    36	  const sb = join(root, 'snapback4');
    37	  const metadata = JSON.parse(command(sb, 'cargo', ['metadata', '--offline', '--no-deps', '--format-version=1']));
    38	  const members = metadata.packages.filter(pkg => metadata.workspace_members.includes(pkg.id));
    39	  const nameByPath = new Map(members.map(pkg => [dirname(pkg.manifest_path), pkg.name]));
    40	  const crates = members.map(pkg => ({
    41	    name: pkg.name,
    42	    dir: slash(relative(root, dirname(pkg.manifest_path))),
    43	    dependencies: sorted(pkg.dependencies.map(dep => nameByPath.get(dep.path)).filter(Boolean)),
    44	    procMacro: pkg.targets.some(target => target.kind.includes('proc-macro')),
    45	    targets: pkg.targets.map(target => ({ name: target.name, kind: target.kind, path: slash(relative(root, target.src_path)), requiredFeatures: target['required-features'] ?? [] })),
    46	  }));
    47	  const files = walk(sb).map(path => PREFIX + path);
    48	  const text = path => existsSync(join(root, path)) ? readFileSync(join(root, path), 'utf8') : '';
    49	  const packages = readdirSync(join(sb, 'packages'), { withFileTypes: true }).filter(entry => entry.isDirectory())
    50	    .filter(entry => existsSync(join(sb, 'packages', entry.name, 'package.json'))).map(entry => {
    51	      const dir = `${PREFIX}packages/${entry.name}`;
    52	      const manifest = JSON.parse(text(`${dir}/package.json`));
    53	      return { name: manifest.name, dir, scripts: manifest.scripts ?? {}, dependencies: sorted(Object.keys({
    54	        ...manifest.dependencies, ...manifest.devDependencies, ...manifest.peerDependencies, ...manifest.optionalDependencies,
    55	      })), tests: files.filter(path => path.startsWith(dir + '/') && /(?:\.test|\.spec)\.[cm]?[jt]sx?$/.test(path)) };
    56	    });
    57	  const fileSet = new Set(files);
    58	  const evidenceCache = new Map();
    59	  function evidence(path, seen = new Set()) {
    60	    if (seen.has(path)) return [];
    61	    seen.add(path);
    62	    const source = text(path);
    63	    const hits = [];
    64	    if (/\/dist\//.test(source)) hits.push(`${path}: client build consumer`);
    65	    if (/child_process|snapback4(?:\.exe)?['"`]|SNAPBACK4_BIN|processDevice|bin-path/.test(source)) hits.push(`${path}: CLI/process consumer`);
    66	    if (/wasm|webDevice/.test(source)) hits.push(`${path}: wasm consumer`);
    67	    // Follow literal imports/re-exports, require(), dynamic import(), and URL resources.
    68	    // Unresolved local imports are conservative consumers of both products.
    69	    const imports = [...source.matchAll(/(?:from\s*|import\s*\(|require\s*\(|new URL\s*\(|import\s*)['"]([^'"\n]+)['"]/g)];
    70	    for (const [, specifier] of imports) {
    71	      if (!specifier.startsWith('.')) continue;
    72	      const raw = slash(relative(root, resolve(root, dirname(path), specifier)));
    73	      const candidates = [raw, raw.replace('/dist/', '/src/').replace(/\.js$/, '.ts'), raw + '.mjs', raw + '.js', raw + '.ts', raw + '/index.ts'];
    74	      const found = candidates.find(candidate => fileSet.has(candidate));
    75	      if (!found) hits.push(`${path}: unresolved local import ${specifier} (CLI + wasm)`);
    76	      else if (/\.[cm]?[jt]sx?$/.test(found)) hits.push(...evidence(found, seen));
    77	    }
    78	    if (/\b(?:import|require)\s*\(\s*[^'"\s]/.test(source)) hits.push(`${path}: computed import (CLI + wasm)`);
    79	    return sorted(hits);
    80	  }
    81	  for (const pkg of packages) for (const test of pkg.tests) evidenceCache.set(test, evidence(test));
    82	  return { crates, packages, files, text, clientEvidence: Object.fromEntries(evidenceCache) };
    83	}
    84	
    85	export function reverseClosure(seeds, nodes) {
    86	  const result = new Set(seeds);
    87	  let changed = true;
    88	  while (changed) {
    89	    changed = false;
    90	    for (const node of nodes) if (!result.has(node.name) && node.dependencies.some(dep => result.has(dep))) {
    91	      result.add(node.name); changed = true;
    92	    }
    93	  }
    94	  return sorted(result);
    95	}

    97	export function selectAffected(changedFiles, model) {
    98	  const files = sorted(changedFiles);
    99	  for (const path of files) if (path.startsWith('/') || path.split('/').includes('..') || path.includes('\0')) throw new Error(`invalid path ${JSON.stringify(path)}`);
   100	  const reasons = [];
   101	  const fullCrates = new Set();
   102	  const sourceCrates = new Set();
   103	  const narrowRust = new Map();
   104	  const fullPackages = new Set();
   105	  const clientFiles = new Set();
   106	  const checks = new Set(files.length ? ['ref-check'] : []);
   107	  let broad = false;
   108	  let semantics = false;
   109	  const why = (path, reason) => reasons.push({ path, reason });
   110	  function widen(path, reason) { broad = true; why(path, `fail broad: ${reason}`); }
   111	  function docs(path) { checks.add('guide'); checks.add('docs-budget'); checks.add('packaged-docs'); why(path, 'guide/docs contract'); }
   112	  for (const path of files) {
   113	    if (path.startsWith('llp/') || /(?:^|\/)AGENTS\.md$|^CLAUDE\.md$/.test(path)) {
   114	      checks.add('ref-check'); why(path, 'LLP/agent instructions'); continue;
   115	    }
   116	    if (!path.startsWith(PREFIX)) {
   117	      if (['scripts/select-local-proof.mjs', 'scripts/local-proof-selection-policy.v1.json', 'scripts/verify-local-proof', 'tests/local_proof_selection_v0.mjs', 'tests/local_proof_inventory_v0.mjs'].includes(path)) { checks.add('selector-tests'); checks.add('inventories'); why(path, 'root selector integration'); }
   118	      else if (path.startsWith('guide/')) docs(path);
   119	      else widen(path, 'unowned path outside Snapback 4');
   120	      continue;
   121	    }
   122	    const p = path.slice(PREFIX.length);
   123	    if (/(?:^|\/)(?:Cargo\.toml|Cargo\.lock|build\.rs|package\.json|bun\.lock|rust-toolchain(?:\.toml)?)$/.test(p) || p.startsWith('.cargo/')) {
   124	      widen(path, 'manifest, lock, toolchain or build script'); continue;
   125	    }
   126	    // Changes to this selector and its direct tests have their own affected suite.
   127	    if (/^scripts\/affected(?:-replay)?(?:\.test)?\.mjs$/.test(p)) {
   128	      checks.add('selector-tests'); why(path, 'selector implementation/fixtures'); continue;
   129	    }
   130	    if (/^crates\/snapback4-lang\/src\/guide(?:\/|\.rs$)/.test(p) || /^(?:guide|docs)\//.test(p) || /\.md$/.test(p)) {
   131	      docs(path); checks.add('ref-check');
   132	      if (/^crates\/snapback4-lang\/src\/guide/.test(p)) {
   133	        if (!narrowRust.has('snapback4-lang')) narrowRust.set('snapback4-lang', new Set());
   134	        narrowRust.get('snapback4-lang').add('guide');
   135	        if (path.endsWith('.rs')) { checks.add('diagnostics'); checks.add('inventories'); }
   136	      }
   137	      continue;
   138	    }
   139	    const crate = model.crates.find(crate => path.startsWith(crate.dir + '/'));
   140	    if (crate) {
   141	      if (crate.procMacro) { widen(path, 'proc-macro'); continue; }
   142	      const test = crate.targets.find(target => target.kind.includes('test') && target.path === path);
   143	      if (test && path.endsWith('.rs')) {
   144	        if (!narrowRust.has(crate.name)) narrowRust.set(crate.name, new Set());
   145	        narrowRust.get(crate.name).add(test.name);
   146	        why(path, `integration test target ${crate.name}::${test.name}`);
   147	      } else if (path.startsWith(crate.dir + '/src/') && /\.(?:rs|q|json)$/.test(path)) {
   148	        sourceCrates.add(crate.name); why(path, `${crate.name} sources and reverse dependents`);
   149	        if (['snapback4-lang', 'snapback4-core'].includes(crate.name)) semantics = true;
   150	        // Site coordinates drift even when no new diagnostic code is introduced.
   151	        checks.add('diagnostics'); checks.add('inventories');
   152	        if (crate.name === 'snapback4-lang') checks.add('generated');
   153	      } else widen(path, 'shared Rust test support, fixture or unowned crate input');
   154	      continue;
   155	    }
   156	    const pkg = model.packages.find(pkg => path.startsWith(pkg.dir + '/'));
   157	    if (pkg) {
   158	      if (pkg.tests.includes(path)) { clientFiles.add(path); why(path, 'client test file only'); }
   159	      else if (path.startsWith(pkg.dir + '/test/') || path.startsWith(pkg.dir + '/tests/')) widen(path, 'shared client test support');
   160	      else { fullPackages.add(pkg.name); why(path, 'client package source and reverse dependents');
   161	        if (!pkg.tests.length && !pkg.scripts.test) widen(path, 'package has no owned test runner; include cross-package consumers');
   162	      }
   163	      continue;
   164	    }
   165	    if (p.startsWith('scripts/') || p.startsWith('fixtures/') || p.startsWith('vendor/') || p.startsWith('native-fixtures/')) widen(path, 'shared runner, fixture or vendored build input');
   166	    else if (p.startsWith('lean/')) { semantics = true; why(path, 'Lean model'); }
   167	    else widen(path, 'unknown Snapback 4 path');
   168	    if (/generated|codegen/.test(path)) checks.add('generated');
   169	  }
   170	  for (const path of files) if (model.text?.(path).includes('@ref')) checks.add('ref-check');
   171	  for (const name of reverseClosure([...sourceCrates], model.crates)) fullCrates.add(name);
   172	  if (broad) {
   173	    for (const crate of model.crates) fullCrates.add(crate.name);
   174	    for (const pkg of model.packages) fullPackages.add(pkg.name);
   175	    for (const check of ['guide', 'docs-budget', 'packaged-docs', 'diagnostics', 'inventories', 'generated', 'ref-check', 'selector-tests']) checks.add(check);
   176	    semantics = true;
   177	  }
   178	  for (const name of reverseClosure([...fullPackages], model.packages)) fullPackages.add(name);
   179	  for (const pkg of model.packages) if (fullPackages.has(pkg.name)) for (const test of pkg.tests) clientFiles.add(test);
   180	  const products = reverseClosure([...sourceCrates], model.crates);
   181	  const cliChanged = broad || products.includes('snapback4');
   182	  const wasmChanged = broad || products.includes('snapback4-device');
   183	  for (const [test, evidence] of Object.entries(model.clientEvidence ?? {})) {
   184	    const matched = evidence.filter(hit => (cliChanged && /CLI|process consumer/.test(hit)) || (wasmChanged && /wasm/.test(hit)));
   185	    if (matched.length) { clientFiles.add(test); for (const hit of matched) why(test, hit); }
   186	  }
   187	  if (wasmChanged) checks.add('wasm-measurement');
   188	  const steps = [];
   189	  const step = (id, cwd, argv, reason) => steps.push({ id, cwd, argv, reason, timeoutSeconds: TIMEOUT });
   190	  const cargo = (id, args, reason) => step(id, 'snapback4', ['cargo', ...args], reason);
   191	  // Build every product used by selected tests, even for a test-only diff; never
   192	  // trust ignored binaries/dist/wasm left over from another revision.
   193	  const evidence = [...clientFiles].flatMap(test => model.clientEvidence?.[test] ?? []);
   194	  const needCli = evidence.some(hit => /CLI|process consumer/.test(hit));
   195	  const needClient = evidence.some(hit => /client build consumer/.test(hit));
   196	  const needWasm = needClient || evidence.some(hit => /wasm/.test(hit));
   197	  if (needCli) cargo('build-cli', ['build', '-p', 'snapback4'], 'selected clients spawn/import CLI');
   198	  if (needWasm) step('build-wasm', 'snapback4', ['bash', 'scripts/build-wasm.sh'], 'selected clients import wasm');
   199	  if (needClient) step('build-client', 'snapback4', ['bun', 'run', '--cwd', 'packages/snapback4', 'build'], 'selected tests import dist');
   200	  for (const pkg of model.packages) if (fullPackages.has(pkg.name)) {
   201	    if (pkg.scripts.typecheck) step(`typecheck:${pkg.name}`, 'snapback4', ['bun', 'run', '--cwd', pkg.dir.slice(PREFIX.length), 'typecheck'], 'package source change');
   202	    if (pkg.scripts.test) step(`package-test:${pkg.name}`, 'snapback4', ['bun', 'run', '--cwd', pkg.dir.slice(PREFIX.length), 'test'], 'package test script');
   203	  }
   204	  // One Cargo call per package/target permits independent failures to be reported;
   205	  // --no-fail-fast additionally preserves Cargo's other targets within a package.
   206	  for (const name of sorted(fullCrates)) {
   207	    const features = sorted(model.crates.find(crate => crate.name === name).targets.flatMap(target => target.requiredFeatures ?? []));
   208	    cargo(`rust:${name}`, ['test', '--no-fail-fast', '-p', name, ...(features.length ? ['--features', features.join(',')] : [])], 'source/reverse dependency closure or fail broad');
   209	  }
   210	  for (const [name, targets] of [...narrowRust].sort()) if (!fullCrates.has(name)) for (const target of sorted(targets)) {
   211	    const features = model.crates.find(crate => crate.name === name).targets.find(item => item.name === target)?.requiredFeatures ?? [];
   212	    cargo(`rust:${name}:${target}`, ['test', '--no-fail-fast', '-p', name, '--test', target, ...(features.length ? ['--features', features.join(',')] : [])], 'only this integration test changed');
   213	  }
   214	  const modelFiles = [...clientFiles].filter(path => /\/(?:app-session-model|online-client-model)\.test\.mjs$/.test(path));
   215	  const ordinaryFiles = sorted([...clientFiles].filter(path => !modelFiles.includes(path)));
   216	  const concurrency = String(process.env.SNAPBACK4_TEST_SERIAL_CLIENT_FILES === '1' ? 1 : Math.min(24, Math.max(1, Math.floor(availableParallelism() / 3))));
   217	  if (ordinaryFiles.length) step('client-tests', 'snapback4', ['node', 'scripts/run-client-tests.mjs', concurrency, 'files', ...ordinaryFiles.map(path => path.slice(PREFIX.length))], 'selected client files (one existing custody pool)');
   218	  if (modelFiles.length) step('client-models', 'snapback4', ['node', 'scripts/run-client-tests.mjs', '1', 'models', ...sorted(modelFiles).map(path => path.slice(PREFIX.length))], 'models retain serial custody');
   219	  const checkCommands = {
   220	    guide: ['.', ['node', 'scripts/check-guide.mjs']],
   221	    'docs-budget': ['snapback4', ['node', 'scripts/docs-budget.mjs']],
   222	    'packaged-docs': ['snapback4', ['node', '--input-type=module', '-e', "import { lintPackagedDocs } from './scripts/packaged-docs.mjs'; lintPackagedDocs(process.cwd());"]],
   223	    diagnostics: ['.', ['node', 'scripts/generate-diagnostics-registry.mjs', '--check']],
   224	    inventories: ['.', ['node', 'scripts/check-inventories.mjs']],
   225	    generated: ['.', ['bash', 'scripts/check-generated-artifacts']],
   226	    'ref-check': ['.', ['./ref-check']],
   227	    'selector-tests': ['.', ['node', '--test', 'snapback4/scripts/affected.test.mjs', 'tests/local_proof_selection_v0.mjs', 'tests/local_proof_inventory_v0.mjs']],
   228	    'wasm-measurement': ['snapback4', ['bash', 'scripts/measure-wasm.sh']],
   229	  };
   230	  for (const check of sorted(checks)) step(check, ...checkCommands[check], 'affected input contract');
   231	  if (semantics) {
   232	    step('lean-build', 'snapback4/lean', ['lake', 'build'], 'semantic oracle prerequisite');
   233	    for (const mode of ['run-lean', 'run']) cargo(`difftest:${mode}`, ['run', '--release', '-p', 'snapback4-difftest', '--', mode, '--seed', '0', '--cases', '2000', '--workers', '2', '--out', `target/affected/difftest-${mode}`], 'language, planner/store semantics or fail broad');
   234	  }
   235	  return { schemaVersion: 'snapback4.affected.v1', files, broad, reasons, rustPackages: sorted(fullCrates),
   236	    rustTargets: Object.fromEntries([...narrowRust].filter(([name]) => !fullCrates.has(name)).map(([name, targets]) => [name, sorted(targets)])),
   237	    clientFiles: sorted(clientFiles), checks: sorted(checks), steps };
   238	}

    30	let submodulePathCache = null;
    31	const POLICY_PATH = resolve(ROOT, 'scripts/local-proof-selection-policy.v1.json');
    32	const PROOF_PATH = resolve(ROOT, 'scripts/verify-local-proof');
    33	
    34	if (process.argv[1] && resolve(process.argv[1]) === fileURLToPath(import.meta.url)) main();
    35	
    36	export function loadSelectionContext() {
    37	  const policyBytes = readFileSync(POLICY_PATH);
    38	  const proofSource = readFileSync(PROOF_PATH, 'utf8');
    39	  const calls = parseGateCalls(proofSource);
    40	  const inapplicabilityDeclarations = parseInapplicabilityDeclarations(proofSource);
    41	  validateInapplicabilityDeclarations(calls, inapplicabilityDeclarations);
    42	  const policy = JSON.parse(policyBytes.toString('utf8'));
    43	  const inventory = validateSelectionPolicy(policy, calls);
    44	  return {
    45	    policy,
    46	    calls,
    47	    inapplicabilityDeclarations,
    48	    inventory,
    49	    policyDigest: sha256(policyBytes),
    50	    gateInventoryDigest: sha256(Buffer.from(JSON.stringify({
    51	      calls,
    52	      inapplicabilityDeclarations,
    53	    }))),
    54	  };
    55	}
    56	
    57	export function validateSelectionPolicy(policy, calls) {
    58	  assert(
    59	    policy?.schemaVersion === 'snapback.local-proof-selection-policy.v1',
    60	    'selection policy schema',
    61	  );
    62	  assert(policy.unknownPathDisposition === 'all', 'unknown paths fail broad');
    63	  const canonicalGates = [...new Set(calls.map(({ gateId }) => gateId))].sort();
    64	  const canonicalSet = new Set(canonicalGates);
    65	  assert(canonicalGates.length > 80, 'canonical gate inventory is non-vacuous');
    66	
    67	  const groupNames = Object.keys(policy.groups ?? {}).sort();
    68	  assert(groupNames.length > 0, 'selection groups exist');
    69	  const declared = new Set();
    70	  const grouped = new Set();
    71	  validateGateList(policy.alwaysGates, 'always gates', canonicalSet, declared);
    72	  for (const name of groupNames) {
    73	    const group = policy.groups[name];
    74	    assert(group?.lane === 'fast' || group?.lane === 'affected', `group ${name} lane`);
    75	    validateGateList(group.gates, `group ${name} gates`, canonicalSet, declared);
    76	    for (const gate of group.gates) grouped.add(gate);
    77	  }
    78	  assert(
    79	    canonicalGates.every((gate) => declared.has(gate)),
    80	    `unclassified canonical gates: ${canonicalGates.filter((gate) => !declared.has(gate)).join(',')}`,
    81	  );
    82	
    83	  validateUniqueStrings(policy.affectedBaselineGroups, 'affected baseline groups');
    84	  for (const group of policy.affectedBaselineGroups) {
    85	    assert(policy.groups[group]?.lane === 'affected', `affected baseline group ${group}`);
    86	  }
    87	
    88	  const ruleIds = new Set();
    89	  assert(Array.isArray(policy.pathRules) && policy.pathRules.length > 0, 'path rules exist');
    90	  for (const rule of policy.pathRules) {
    91	    assert(typeof rule?.id === 'string' && /^[a-z0-9-]+$/u.test(rule.id), 'path rule id');
    92	    assert(!ruleIds.has(rule.id), `duplicate path rule ${rule.id}`);
    93	    ruleIds.add(rule.id);
    94	    const matchers = ['files', 'prefixes', 'suffixes', 'patterns']
    95	      .flatMap((key) => rule[key] ?? []);
    96	    validateUniqueStrings(matchers, `path rule ${rule.id} matchers`);
    97	    assert(matchers.length > 0, `path rule ${rule.id} has a matcher`);
    98	    validateRuleExtensions(rule, `path rule ${rule.id}`);
    99	    validateRulePatterns(rule, `path rule ${rule.id}`);
   100	    if (rule.disposition === 'all') {
   101	      assert(rule.groups === undefined, `broad path rule ${rule.id} has no groups`);
   102	    } else {
   103	      assert(rule.disposition === undefined, `path rule ${rule.id} disposition`);
   104	      validateUniqueStrings(rule.groups, `path rule ${rule.id} groups`);
   105	      for (const group of rule.groups) assert(policy.groups[group], `path rule ${rule.id} group ${group}`);
   106	    }
   107	  }
   108	  assert(Array.isArray(policy.additivePathRules), 'additive path rules exist');
   109	  for (const rule of policy.additivePathRules) {
   110	    assert(typeof rule?.id === 'string' && /^[a-z0-9-]+$/u.test(rule.id), 'additive path rule id');
   111	    assert(!ruleIds.has(rule.id), `duplicate path rule ${rule.id}`);
   112	    ruleIds.add(rule.id);
   113	    const matchers = ['files', 'prefixes', 'suffixes', 'patterns']
   114	      .flatMap((key) => rule[key] ?? []);
   115	    validateUniqueStrings(matchers, `additive path rule ${rule.id} matchers`);
   116	    assert(matchers.length > 0, `additive path rule ${rule.id} has a matcher`);
   117	    validateRuleExtensions(rule, `additive path rule ${rule.id}`);
   118	    validateRulePatterns(rule, `additive path rule ${rule.id}`);
   119	    assert(rule.disposition === undefined, `additive path rule ${rule.id} disposition`);
   120	    validateUniqueStrings(rule.groups, `additive path rule ${rule.id} groups`);
   121	    for (const group of rule.groups) {
   122	      assert(policy.groups[group], `additive path rule ${rule.id} group ${group}`);
   123	    }
   124	  }
   125	
   126	  for (const [gate, prerequisites] of Object.entries(policy.dependencyOverrides ?? {})) {
   127	    assert(canonicalSet.has(gate), `dependency override gate ${gate}`);
   128	    validateUniqueStrings(prerequisites, `dependency override ${gate}`);
   129	    for (const prerequisite of prerequisites) {
   130	      assert(canonicalSet.has(prerequisite), `dependency override ${gate} -> ${prerequisite}`);
   131	    }
   132	  }
   133	  for (const [name, markers] of Object.entries(policy.workflowRequirementMarkers ?? {})) {
   134	    validateUniqueStrings(markers, `workflow requirement ${name}`);
   135	    for (const gate of markers) assert(canonicalSet.has(gate), `workflow marker ${name}:${gate}`);
   136	  }
   137	  const driftEntries = Object.entries(policy.driftProneGates ?? {});
   138	  assert(driftEntries.length > 0, 'drift-prone gate registry exists');
   139	  for (const [gate, metadata] of driftEntries) {
   140	    assert(canonicalSet.has(gate), `drift-prone gate ${gate}`);
   141	    assert(grouped.has(gate), `drift-prone gate ${gate} belongs to an affected path group`);
   142	    validateUniqueStrings(metadata?.records, `drift-prone gate ${gate} records`);
   143	    validateUniqueStrings(metadata?.recordPaths, `drift-prone gate ${gate} record paths`);
   144	    for (const path of metadata.recordPaths) {
   145	      assert(validRepositoryPath(path), `drift-prone gate ${gate} record path ${path}`);
   146	    }
   147	    const triggerMatchers = ['files', 'prefixes', 'suffixes']
   148	      .flatMap((key) => metadata?.triggerRules?.[key] ?? []);
   149	    validateUniqueStrings(triggerMatchers, `drift-prone gate ${gate} trigger rules`);
   150	    assert(triggerMatchers.length > 0, `drift-prone gate ${gate} has trigger rules`);
   151	    for (const matcher of triggerMatchers) {
   152	      assert(validRepositoryPath(matcher), `drift-prone gate ${gate} trigger ${matcher}`);
   153	    }
   154	    assert(
   155	      typeof metadata.regenerateCommand === 'string' && metadata.regenerateCommand.length > 0,
   156	      `drift-prone gate ${gate} regenerate command`,
   157	    );
   158	    if (metadata.blessedRegenerateCommand !== undefined) {
   159	      assert(
   160	        typeof metadata.blessedRegenerateCommand === 'string'
   161	          && metadata.blessedRegenerateCommand.length > 0,
   162	        `drift-prone gate ${gate} blessed regenerate command`,
   163	      );
   164	    }
   165	    if (metadata.prepareCommand !== undefined) {
   166	      assert(
   167	        typeof metadata.prepareCommand === 'string' && metadata.prepareCommand.length > 0,
   168	        `drift-prone gate ${gate} prepare command`,
   169	      );
   170	    }
   171	    if (metadata.requiredModules !== undefined) {
   172	      validateUniqueStrings(metadata.requiredModules, `drift-prone gate ${gate} required modules`);
   173	      for (const module of metadata.requiredModules) {
   174	        assert(
   175	          /^[A-Za-z0-9@][A-Za-z0-9@._/-]*$/u.test(module) && !module.includes('..'),
   176	          `drift-prone gate ${gate} required module ${module}`,
   177	        );
   178	      }
   179	    }
   180	    if (metadata.requiredEnvironmentPaths !== undefined) {
   181	      validateUniqueStrings(
   182	        metadata.requiredEnvironmentPaths,
   183	        `drift-prone gate ${gate} required environment paths`,
   184	      );
   185	      for (const path of metadata.requiredEnvironmentPaths) {
   186	        assert(
   187	          validRepositoryPath(path),
   188	          `drift-prone gate ${gate} required environment path ${path}`,
   189	        );
   190	      }
   191	    }
   192	    if (metadata.requiredSubmodules !== undefined) {
   193	      validateUniqueStrings(
   194	        metadata.requiredSubmodules,
   195	        `drift-prone gate ${gate} required submodules`,
   196	      );
   197	      for (const path of metadata.requiredSubmodules) {
   198	        assert(
   199	          validRepositoryPath(path),
   200	          `drift-prone gate ${gate} required submodule ${path}`,
   201	        );
   202	      }
   203	    }
   204	    if (metadata.sameChangeSemantics !== undefined) {
   205	      assert(
   206	        metadata.sameChangeSemantics === 'historical-validity-only-per-llp-0175',
   207	        `drift-prone gate ${gate} same-change semantics`,
   208	      );
   209	    }
   210	  }
   211	  const driftGateIds = new Set(driftEntries.map(([gate]) => gate));
   212	  validateUniqueStrings(policy.driftAlwaysRunGates, 'drift always-run gates');
   213	  assert(policy.driftAlwaysRunGates.length > 0, 'drift always-run gates are non-empty');
   214	  for (const gate of policy.driftAlwaysRunGates) {
   215	    assert(driftGateIds.has(gate), `drift always-run gate ${gate}`);
   216	  }
   217	  validateUniqueStrings(policy.driftFailClosedCoreGates, 'drift fail-closed core gates');
   218	  assert(policy.driftFailClosedCoreGates.length > 0, 'drift fail-closed core gates are non-empty');
   219	  for (const gate of policy.driftFailClosedCoreGates) {
   220	    assert(driftGateIds.has(gate), `drift fail-closed core gate ${gate}`);
   221	  }
   222	
   223	  return { canonicalGates, groupNames };
   224	}
   225	
   226	export function selectChangedPaths(changedFiles, context = loadSelectionContext()) {
   227	  const { policy, calls, inventory } = context;
   228	  validateUniqueStrings(changedFiles, 'changed files');
   229	  const classifications = [];
   230	  const selectedGroups = new Set();
   231	  const matchedRules = new Set();
   232	  const broadFiles = [];
   233	  const unknownFiles = [];
   234	
   235	  for (const path of [...changedFiles].sort()) {
   236	    assert(validRepositoryPath(path), `invalid changed path ${JSON.stringify(path)}`);
   237	    const rule = policy.pathRules.find((candidate) => matchesRule(path, candidate));
   238	    const additiveRules = policy.additivePathRules
   239	      .filter((candidate) => matchesRule(path, candidate));
   240	    const additiveRuleIds = additiveRules.map(({ id }) => id).sort();
   241	    const additiveGroups = additiveRules.flatMap(({ groups }) => groups);
   242	    for (const { id } of additiveRules) matchedRules.add(id);
   243	    for (const group of additiveGroups) selectedGroups.add(group);
   244	    if (!rule) {
   245	      unknownFiles.push(path);
   246	      classifications.push({
   247	        path,
   248	        ruleId: null,
   249	        additiveRuleIds,
   250	        disposition: 'all',
   251	        groups: [...new Set(additiveGroups)].sort(),
   252	      });
   253	      continue;
   254	    }
   255	    matchedRules.add(rule.id);
   256	    const disposition = rule.disposition === 'all' ? 'all' : 'groups';
   257	    const groups = [...new Set([...(rule.groups ?? []), ...additiveGroups])].sort();
   258	    if (disposition === 'all') broadFiles.push(path);
   259	    for (const group of groups) selectedGroups.add(group);
   260	    classifications.push({ path, ruleId: rule.id, additiveRuleIds, disposition, groups });
   261	  }
   262	
   263	  const failBroad = broadFiles.length > 0 || unknownFiles.length > 0;
   264	  if (failBroad) {
   265	    for (const group of inventory.groupNames) selectedGroups.add(group);
   266	  }
   267	  const hasAffectedGroup = [...selectedGroups]
   268	    .some((group) => policy.groups[group].lane === 'affected');
   269	  // Snapback 4 has independent workspaces; legacy Exact/admin is not its baseline.
   270	  if ([...selectedGroups].some(group => group !== 'snapback4' && policy.groups[group].lane === 'affected')) {
   271	    for (const group of policy.affectedBaselineGroups) selectedGroups.add(group);
   272	  }
   273	
   274	  const selected = new Set(policy.alwaysGates);
   275	  for (const group of selectedGroups) {
   276	    for (const gate of policy.groups[group].gates) selected.add(gate);
   277	  }
   278	  closePrerequisites(selected, calls, policy.dependencyOverrides ?? {});
   279	  if (failBroad) for (const gate of inventory.canonicalGates) selected.add(gate);
   280	
   281	  const selectedGates = inventory.canonicalGates.filter((gate) => selected.has(gate));
   282	  const notSelectedGates = inventory.canonicalGates.filter((gate) => !selected.has(gate));
   283	  const selectedSet = new Set(selectedGates);
   284	  const markers = policy.workflowRequirementMarkers;
   285	  const requires = (name) => markers[name].some((gate) => selectedSet.has(gate));
   286	  const selectionClass = failBroad
   287	    ? 'FAIL_CLOSED_FULL'
   288	    : hasAffectedGroup
   289	      ? 'AFFECTED'
   290	      : 'FAST_ONLY';
   291	
   292	  return {
   293	    selectionClass,
   294	    classifications,
   295	    matchedRules: [...matchedRules].sort(),
   296	    selectedGroups: [...selectedGroups].sort(),
   297	    broadFiles,
   298	    unknownFiles,
   299	    selectedGates,
   300	    notSelectedGates,
   301	    requirements: {
   302	      // @ref LLP 0175#d2--assign-every-test-to-an-explicit-cadence — the
   303	      // docs-only core stays dependency-free; fast path-owned gates declare
   304	      // the narrow workflow setup they need instead of widening the core.
   305	      javascriptDependencies: selectionClass !== 'FAST_ONLY'
   306	        || selectedSet.has('generated-artifacts')
   307	        || requires('javascriptDependencies'),
   308	      native: requires('native'),
   309	      rustToolchain: requires('native') || requires('rustToolchain'),
   310	      browser: requires('browser'),
   311	      cargoAudit: requires('cargoAudit'),
   312	      privateRoot: requires('privateRoot'),
   313	    },
   314	  };
   315	}
   316	
   317	export function selectDriftProneGates(
   318	  changedFiles,
   319	  context = loadSelectionContext(),
   320	  { failClosedDisposition = 'core' } = {},
   321	) {
   322	  assert(
   323	    failClosedDisposition === 'core' || failClosedDisposition === 'full',
   324	    `drift fail-closed disposition ${failClosedDisposition}`,
   325	  );
   326	  const selection = selectChangedPaths(changedFiles, context);
   327	  const driftGateIds = new Set(Object.keys(context.policy.driftProneGates));
   328	  const selected = new Set(context.policy.driftAlwaysRunGates);
   329	  for (const gate of driftGateIds) {
   330	    if (changedFiles.some((path) => (
   331	      matchesRule(path, context.policy.driftProneGates[gate].triggerRules)
   332	    ))) selected.add(gate);
   333	  }
   334	  const driftFailClosedDisposition = selection.selectionClass === 'FAIL_CLOSED_FULL'
   335	    ? failClosedDisposition
   336	    : null;
   337	  if (driftFailClosedDisposition === 'full') {
   338	    for (const gate of driftGateIds) selected.add(gate);
   339	  } else if (driftFailClosedDisposition === 'core') {
   340	    for (const gate of context.policy.driftFailClosedCoreGates) selected.add(gate);
   341	  }
   342	  const selectedDriftProneGates = context.inventory.canonicalGates.filter((gate) => (
   343	    selected.has(gate)
   344	  ));
   345	  return { ...selection, driftFailClosedDisposition, selectedDriftProneGates };
   346	}
   347	
   348	export function completeSelection(context = loadSelectionContext()) {
   349	  const { inventory, policy } = context;
   350	  const selectedSet = new Set(inventory.canonicalGates);
   351	  const markers = policy.workflowRequirementMarkers;
   352	  const requires = (name) => markers[name].some((gate) => selectedSet.has(gate));
   353	  return {
   354	    selectionClass: 'COMPLETE',
   355	    classifications: [],
   356	    matchedRules: [],
   357	    selectedGroups: inventory.groupNames,
   358	    broadFiles: [],
   359	    unknownFiles: [],
   360	    selectedGates: inventory.canonicalGates,
   361	    notSelectedGates: [],
   362	    requirements: {
   363	      javascriptDependencies: true,
   364	      native: requires('native'),
   365	      rustToolchain: requires('native') || requires('rustToolchain'),
   366	      browser: requires('browser'),
   367	      cargoAudit: requires('cargoAudit'),
   368	      privateRoot: requires('privateRoot'),
   369	    },
   370	  };
   371	}
   372	
   373	export function selectGate(gateId, context = loadSelectionContext()) {
   374	  assert(/^[a-z0-9-]+$/u.test(gateId), `invalid gate id ${JSON.stringify(gateId)}`);
   375	  assert(context.inventory.canonicalGates.includes(gateId), `unknown gate ${gateId}`);
   376	  const selected = new Set([gateId]);
   377	  closePrerequisites(selected, context.calls, context.policy.dependencyOverrides ?? {});
   378	  return context.inventory.canonicalGates.filter((gate) => selected.has(gate));
   379	}

   240	export function changedPaths(root, base, head, worktree = false) {
   241	  const committed = paths(git(root, 'diff', '--name-only', '-z', '--no-renames', `${base}...${head}`));
   242	  return sorted([...committed, ...(worktree ? [
   243	    ...paths(git(root, 'diff', '--name-only', '-z', '--no-renames', 'HEAD')),
   244	    ...paths(git(root, 'ls-files', '--others', '--exclude-standard', '-z')),
   245	  ] : [])]);
   246	}
   247	function snapshotDigest(root, files) {
   248	  return hash(JSON.stringify(files.map(path => [path, existsSync(join(root, path)) ? hash(readFileSync(join(root, path))) : null])));
   249	}

   251	// Historical planning reads the selected revision's Cargo graph and test sources.
   252	// The scratch snapshot is confined to this worktree's target and never runs tests.
   253	export function modelAtRevision(root, revision, fn) {
   254	  const parent = join(root, 'target/affected'); mkdirSync(parent, { recursive: true });
   255	  const snapshot = mkdtempSync(join(parent, 'snapshot-'));
   256	  try {
   257	    const tree = git(root, 'ls-tree', '-r', '--name-only', revision, '--', 'snapback4').trim().split('\n');
   258	    const wanted = ['snapback4/Cargo.toml', 'snapback4/Cargo.lock', 'snapback4/package.json', 'snapback4/crates', 'snapback4/packages', 'snapback4/scripts'];
   259	    const present = wanted.filter(path => tree.some(file => file === path || file.startsWith(path + '/')));
   260	    const archive = command(root, 'git', ['archive', revision, '--', ...present], { encoding: null });
   261	    command(snapshot, 'tar', ['-xf', '-'], { input: archive });
   262	    return fn(loadModel(snapshot));
   263	  } finally { rmSync(snapshot, { recursive: true, force: true }); }
   264	}
   265	export function planAffected({ root = ROOT, base = 'origin/main', head = 'HEAD', worktree = false, files } = {}) {
   266	  const headRevision = git(root, 'rev-parse', '--verify', `${head}^{commit}`).trim();
   267	  const baseRevision = git(root, 'rev-parse', '--verify', `${base}^{commit}`).trim();
   268	  const observedFiles = changedPaths(root, baseRevision, headRevision, worktree);
   269	  const changed = files ?? observedFiles;
   270	  const current = git(root, 'rev-parse', 'HEAD').trim();
   271	  if (worktree && headRevision !== current) throw new Error('--worktree requires current HEAD');
   272	  const select = model => selectAffected(changed, model);
   273	  const plan = worktree ? select(loadModel(root)) : modelAtRevision(root, headRevision, select);
   274	  return { ...plan, base: baseRevision, mergeBase: git(root, 'merge-base', baseRevision, headRevision).trim(), head: headRevision,
   275	    worktree, observedFiles, inputDigest: snapshotDigest(root, observedFiles) };
   276	}
   277	const quote = value => `'${value.replaceAll("'", "'\\''")}'`;
   278	export function formatStep(step) {
   279	  return `(cd ${quote(step.cwd)} && timeout ${step.timeoutSeconds}s env ${Object.entries(CARGO_ENV).map(([key, value]) => `${key}=${value}`).join(' ')} ${step.argv.map(quote).join(' ')})`;
   280	}
   281	export async function runPlan(plan, { root = ROOT } = {}) {
   282	  if (git(root, 'rev-parse', 'HEAD').trim() !== plan.head) throw new Error('--run requires the selected HEAD checked out');
   283	  if (!plan.worktree && changedPaths(root, plan.head, plan.head, true).length) throw new Error('dirty worktree: use --worktree to bind uncommitted changes');
   284	  const started = new Date().toISOString();
   285	  const receiptDir = join(root, 'target/affected'); mkdirSync(receiptDir, { recursive: true });
   286	  const receiptPath = join(receiptDir, `${plan.head.slice(0, 12)}-${Date.now()}.json`);
   287	  const receipt = { ...plan, started, results: [], complete: false };
   288	  const persist = () => writeFileSync(receiptPath, JSON.stringify(receipt, null, 2) + '\n');
   289	  persist();
   290	  for (const step of plan.steps) {
   291	    console.error(`== ${step.id}\n${formatStep(step)}`);
   292	    const before = Date.now();
   293	    const result = await new Promise(resolveResult => {
   294	      const child = spawn('timeout', ['--kill-after=10s', `${step.timeoutSeconds}s`, ...step.argv], {
   295	        cwd: resolve(root, step.cwd), env: { ...process.env, ...CARGO_ENV }, stdio: 'inherit',
   296	      });
   297	      child.on('error', error => resolveResult({ status: null, error: error.message }));
   298	      child.on('close', (status, signal) => resolveResult({ status, signal }));
   299	    });
   300	    receipt.results.push({ id: step.id, ...result, elapsedMs: Date.now() - before }); persist();
   301	  }
   302	  receipt.complete = true;
   303	  receipt.finished = new Date().toISOString();
   304	  receipt.unchanged = git(root, 'rev-parse', 'HEAD').trim() === plan.head && snapshotDigest(root, plan.observedFiles) === plan.inputDigest
   305	    && JSON.stringify(changedPaths(root, plan.base, plan.head, plan.worktree)) === JSON.stringify(plan.observedFiles);
   306	  receipt.ok = receipt.unchanged && receipt.results.every(result => result.status === 0);
   307	  persist(); console.error(`Receipt: ${receiptPath}; ${receipt.ok ? 'PASS' : 'FAIL'}`);
   308	  return { receiptPath, receipt };
   309	}
   310	async function main() {
   311	  const args = process.argv.slice(2);
   312	  const options = {}; let json = false; let run = false; let proofReceipt;
   313	  for (let i = 0; i < args.length; i++) {
   314	    const arg = args[i];
   315	    if (arg === '--json') json = true;
   316	    else if (arg === '--run') run = true;
   317	    else if (arg === '--complete') options.files = ['snapback4/Cargo.toml'];
   318	    else if (arg === '--worktree') options.worktree = true;
   319	    else if (['--base', '--head', '--proof-receipt'].includes(arg)) {
   320	      if (!args[i + 1] || args[i + 1].startsWith('--')) throw new Error(`${arg} requires a value`);
   321	      if (arg === '--proof-receipt') proofReceipt = args[++i]; else options[arg.slice(2)] = args[++i];
   322	    } else throw new Error(`unknown argument: ${arg}`);
   323	  }
   324	  if (options.worktree && options.head) throw new Error('--head and --worktree are mutually exclusive');
   325	  if (proofReceipt) {
   326	    const receipt = JSON.parse(readFileSync(proofReceipt, 'utf8'));
   327	    options.base = receipt.requestedBaseRevision ?? receipt.headRevision;
   328	    options.head = receipt.headRevision;
   329	    options.worktree = receipt.worktreeIncluded;
   330	    options.files = receipt.cadence === 'complete' ? ['snapback4/Cargo.toml'] : receipt.changedFiles.filter(path => path.startsWith(PREFIX));
   331	  }
   332	  const plan = planAffected(options);
   333	  if (json) console.log(JSON.stringify(plan, null, 2));

   335	    for (const { path, reason } of plan.reasons) console.log(`# ${path}: ${reason}`);
   336	    for (const step of plan.steps) console.log(formatStep(step));
   337	    console.log(`# ${plan.steps.length} commands, ${plan.rustPackages.length} whole Rust packages, ${plan.clientFiles.length} client files; broad=${plan.broad}`);
   338	  }
   339	  if (run) { const { receipt } = await runPlan(plan); if (!receipt.ok) process.exitCode = 1; }
   340	}

# LLP 0189: Verification cadence and claim gating

**Type:** Decision
**Status:** Active
**Systems:** Testing, CI, Packaging, Documentation, Developer experience, LLP governance
**Author:** Charlie Cheever / Claude (Fable 5)
**Date:** 2026-08-07
**Revised:** 2026-08-25 (Active: linked into `llp/foundation/` as part of the
LLP v2 larger kernel)
**Revised:** 2026-08-10 (D-8 coverage-shape amendment, author-directed in
session, Charlie Cheever: system behavior is a cross-product of axes while
tests and docs pin points; two same-day defects from Diary 0074's probe sat
in uncovered cells — sessions-mode 3× frame delivery, shipping in card-game,
and the keyed-surface ephemeral-link omission. D-8 adds cross-cell parity
evidence on the battery cadence, named boot refusals for combinations a

unchanged.) 2026-08-07 (round-1 dual-family review amendments — mechanism

## Context

`main` lands continuously — the median gap between commits is about 0.1
minutes (LLP 0175's measurement), and many concurrent agent sessions push
around the clock. LLP 0175 already fixed the *CI* half of this problem: pushes
get fast/affected verdicts, and the complete canonical inventory runs on a
six-hour scheduled latest-main cadence instead of per commit.

But the *claim and publication* layer still binds exact commits, and that
layer keeps failing the same way:

- **The C27–C33 qualification loop (2026-08-05/06).** Seven successive Track B
  candidate cuts were abandoned because "the mandatory post-review fetch found
  newer main" before performance could run. The loop was broken only by an
  author ruling (recorded in
  `issues/shelved/20260803-0163-public-operation-identity-carrier.md`) that dual-READY
  reviews survive normal merges.
- **The derived-fork publication withdrawal (2026-08-07).** The
  `operating.instance-forks` claim landed at `7cada777b` after twelve review
  rounds — most of them spent hardening the publication gate rather than the
  feature — and was withdrawn about seven hours later (`a5465b4a2`) because
  wave landings changed nine proof/checker/metrology files inside its
  qualification digest. No fork runtime code changed. The claim died of
  unrelated churn.
- **The 433-commit red gate (2026-08-06).** An exact-count leaf-set assertion
  that any landing could invalidate went red and stayed red for 433 commits,
  blocking the npm promotion's "green local proof at the exact candidate SHA"
  precondition (that promotion has since closed) for every commit in the
  range.
- **Queued, overlapping verification.** Full proofs, qualification cycles, and
  review loops each take longer than the landing gap, so processes queue,
  overlap, and invalidate one another instead of converging.

The common defect: processes that gate landing, claims, or promotion on
*complete verification bound to an exact commit* are structurally incompatible
with this repository's landing cadence. Verification per commit must be cheap;
completeness must come from a schedule; claims must be snapshots that
unrelated landings cannot kill.

## Decision

Ruled by the author (Charlie Cheever), 2026-08-07. These are repo-wide
defaults; LLP 0005's honesty rules stay fully in force.

**D-1 — The scheduled complete battery is the authoritative verdict.** The
complete canonical inventory (LLP 0175 D2.3) runs on schedule — at least
twice daily; the current six-hour cadence satisfies this — and on demand.
Its red results become fix-forward filesystem tickets (one per root failure,
idempotent per still-open ticket, following the
`scripts/drift-prone-gates.mjs backstop` pattern) that the fleet fixes
promptly. A battery red does not retroactively invalidate unrelated landings
or standing claims; it names work. Tickets key on the root-failure signature,
not just the gate: each distinct signature gets its own ticket (the filename
carries a stable signature slug alongside the gate), a re-observation of an
unchanged signature updates its existing open ticket, and a new distinct
failure of an already-ticketed gate is never silently coalesced. When a red gate is
claim-relevant to a standing published claim (its D-4 scoped surface), the
ticket names that claim and the claim record names the open ticket while it
stands — "standing, with known red" is a visible state, and it feeds D-3's
triage. A red persisting across four consecutive scheduled runs escalates to
the author.

**D-2 — Landing gates stay fast and affected; frozen-record verdicts are
deferred by default.** Ordinary landings run the LLP 0175 D2.1/D2.2
fast/affected product checks. The pre-push hook still selects and reports the
frozen-record gates affected by the complete ref-update stream, but does not
execute them unless strict mode is explicitly requested. For revisions
targeting `origin/main`, their ordinary verdict moves to the scheduled LLP 0175
drift backstop, whose reds become
visible, attributable fix-forward tickets under D-1/LLP 0232. There is no
ambient same-change frozen-record blocking requirement.

Non-main ref updates are reported but are neither executed nor described as
scheduled in ordinary mode; a later `origin/main` landing is selected from its
own update. The backstop repeats clean content reds under LLP 0232 D-3 before
attribution, records contradictory determinate executions as nondeterminism
without a causal landing, leaves merge topologies that cannot support a
first-parent claim unattributed, and retains runner-setup self-health handling.

If the affected tests passed before integration, the tested semantic delta is
unchanged after merge/rebase, and integration required no semantic conflict
resolution, advancement of `main` alone does not require rerunning expensive
checks. Changed semantic inputs rerun their affected checks; known failing
affected product tests still require attention. No process, gate, or
qualification loop may require a complete battery at an exact commit as a
precondition for ordinary landing. Explicit strict drift enforcement and the
qualified protocols for publication, promotion, destructive migrations,
urgent security exposure, active destructive corruption, and similar external
effects remain available and blocking where their governing decisions require
it. (LLP 0175 implements the cadence; LLP 0232 owns landing economics.)

### Temporary low-noise development posture (2026-08-24)

Ruled by the author (Charlie Cheever), 2026-08-24. This is an explicit,
reversible operational exception to D-2 while direct pushes arrive in bursts
and known baseline failures make per-push GitHub Actions repeat an already
known verdict. In the measured hour preceding the ruling, 16 pushes produced
130 workflow runs: 66 failed and 40 were cancelled.

- `ref-check` remains on every push, and already path-scoped platform proof
  remains affected by path.
- `local-proof` remains on pull requests and on its six-hour/manual complete
  cadence, but not on every direct `main` push.
- heavyweight cold-clone, required-surface, and Linux-package proofs become
  explicit qualification actions rather than per-push actions.
- deployment-identity and generated-inventory checks remain available on pull
  requests and by explicit dispatch while their current baseline reds are
  repaired; restoration must add reviewed path selection rather than restoring
  unconditional main-push fan-out.
- visibility observation and reconciliation become explicit-only until their
  protected environment, signing material, and external custody exist.
  LLP 0234's fail-closed consequence is unchanged: no visibility widening may
  activate without the protected verdict.

No test, assertion, or qualification command is deleted. Publication,
promotion, destructive migration, urgent-security, active-corruption, and
other irreversible/high-danger protocols retain their own blocking gates.
Restoration is tracked in
`issues/shelved/20260824-restore-automatic-actions-after-low-noise-interval.md` and
requires an author-directed end to this exception.

**D-3 — Claims are snapshots.** A capability claim, publication, or promotion
binds the qualification commit and the scoped evidence surface it names
(D-4). It remains valid until a claim-relevant change lands without
requalification. Unrelated landings — including changes to wave-shared proof
harnesses, metrology, fixtures, or documentation — never invalidate a claim.
Withdrawal is warranted only when (a) the scoped surface changed without
requalification, or (b) the claim is discovered to be factually wrong. A
battery red on the claim's own claim-relevant gates is prima facie (b)
evidence and must be triaged through its cross-referenced ticket (D-1); a red
anywhere else never touches the claim. When the publication validator detects
(a), demotion is mandatory, not discretionary: within one battery period the
claim's advertised state drops to its prior honest tier or gains an explicit
requalification-pending marker naming the ticket, and that ticket names an
owner. A requalification-pending marker outstanding across four consecutive
scheduled batteries escalates to the author, exactly like a persistent
battery red (D-1). Editing a published claim's digest scope is itself a
scoped-surface change: a re-scope takes effect only with requalification or
fresh named-author acceptance binding the new scope. Requalification then happens
on the next natural cycle — the claim's normal qualification protocol at the
next scheduled opportunity — not as an emergency that races the fleet.

**D-3 clarifying amendment — 2026-08-09 (author ruling, Charlie Cheever in
session).** For an already **PUBLISHED** derived-fork record, post-C evolution
of the bound scoped source does not itself refuse or withdraw the publication.
The publication oracle reports the record as published with
`requalificationDue: { scopedChanges, since: C }`, emits a conspicuous warning,
and exits successfully so the drift-backstop pattern can ticket the next
natural requalification cycle. This applies only to post-C evolution. Any
inconsistency in the frozen claim chain — including digest, scope, acceptance,
receipt, or evidence binding mismatch; artifact tampering; historical-byte
rewrite; or C not being an ancestor — remains a hard refusal exactly as before.
Withheld-record behavior is unchanged.

**D-4 — Qualification digests are scoped to the claim.** A qualification
digest covers the claim-relevant surface only: the runtime/implementation of
the claimed capability plus the checkers that define that claim's semantics.
Wave-shared proof harnesses, metrology, and fixture files that neither define
nor affect the claim's semantics move out of claim digests and are protected
instead by their own frozen-record drift gates (the
LLP 0175 `driftProneGates` registry pattern; the R12-1 per-file gate in
`issues/20260807-codegen-declaration-has-no-drift-gate.md` is the model).
Coverage moves; it never vanishes: removing a file from a digest is legal
only in the same change that adds (or names the existing) drift gate covering
it. Scoping is by semantic role, not file category: a checker that defines
the claim's semantics stays in the digest even when wave-shared, and the
digest closes over the semantics-affecting inputs of its in-digest checkers,
pinned by content in the claim record. The scope classification is itself
part of the claim record the named author accepts. A replacement drift gate
must meet LLP 0175's admission bar — a genuinely recomputed value compared

#!/usr/bin/env bash
# The blocking suite. Every step runs; every failure is reported; exit 1 if any failed.
# Budget: 60 s total (rules/RULES.md, adopted by LLP 3000 §4).
set -u
cd "$(dirname "$0")/.."
failures=()
child_pids=()
client_test_concurrency=1
parallel_dir=$(mktemp -d "${TMPDIR:-/tmp}/snapback4-test.XXXXXX")

cleanup() {
  local status=$?
  trap - EXIT INT TERM
  for pid in "${child_pids[@]:-}"; do
    [ -n "$pid" ] || continue
    kill "$pid" 2>/dev/null || true
  done
  for pid in "${child_pids[@]:-}"; do
    [ -n "$pid" ] || continue
    wait "$pid" 2>/dev/null || true
  done
  rm -rf "$parallel_dir"
  exit "$status"
}
trap cleanup EXIT
trap 'exit 130' INT
trap 'exit 143' TERM

run() {
  local name="$1" elapsed=$SECONDS
  shift
  echo "== $name"
  if "$@"; then
    echo "-- ok: $name ($((SECONDS - elapsed)) s)"
  else
    echo "-- FAIL: $name ($((SECONDS - elapsed)) s)"
    failures+=("$name")
  fi
}

stop_logged_command() {
  local signal="$1" status="$2"
  trap '' INT TERM
  kill -s "$signal" "$command_pid" 2>/dev/null || true
  wait "$command_pid" 2>/dev/null || true
  exit "$status"
}

run_logged() {
  local key="$1" name="$2" elapsed=$SECONDS command_pid command_status=0
  shift 2
  echo "== $name" >"$parallel_dir/$key.log"
  "$@" >>"$parallel_dir/$key.log" 2>&1 &
  command_pid=$!
  trap 'stop_logged_command INT 130' INT
  trap 'stop_logged_command TERM 143' TERM
  wait "$command_pid" || command_status=$?
  trap - INT TERM
  if [ "$command_status" = 0 ]; then
    echo "-- ok: $name ($((SECONDS - elapsed)) s)" >>"$parallel_dir/$key.log"
    printf 'ok\n' >"$parallel_dir/$key.status"
  else
    echo "-- FAIL: $name ($((SECONDS - elapsed)) s)" >>"$parallel_dir/$key.log"
    printf 'fail\n' >"$parallel_dir/$key.status"
  fi
}

stop_client_commands() {
  local signal="$1" status="$2" pid
  trap '' INT TERM
  for pid in "${client_pids[@]:-}"; do
    [ -n "$pid" ] || continue
    kill -s "$signal" "$pid" 2>/dev/null || true
  done
  for pid in "${client_pids[@]:-}"; do
    [ -n "$pid" ] || continue
    wait "$pid" 2>/dev/null || true
  done
  exit "$status"
}

run_client_tests() {
  local client_status=0 path pid
  local client_files=()
  local client_pids=()
  trap 'stop_client_commands INT 130' INT
  trap 'stop_client_commands TERM 143' TERM
  for path in packages/snapback4/test/*.test.mjs; do
    case "$(basename "$path")" in
      app-session-model.test.mjs|online-client-model.test.mjs) ;;
      *) client_files+=("$path") ;;
    esac
  done
  echo "-- client test files: ${#client_files[@]}; concurrency: $client_test_concurrency"
  # Progress watchdog: 15-minute floor; largest single guard plus 25% or
  # one minute of slack (run-client-tests.mjs), also in serial mode.
  # Cleanup kills the file's group, then requires two empty group inspections.
  node scripts/run-client-tests.mjs "$client_test_concurrency" files "${client_files[@]}" &
  client_pids+=("$!")
  for pid in "${client_pids[@]}"; do
    wait "$pid" || client_status=1
  done
  trap - INT TERM
  return "$client_status"
}

collect() {
  local key="$1" name="$2" status
  if [ -f "$parallel_dir/$key.log" ]; then
    cat "$parallel_dir/$key.log"
  fi
  if [ -f "$parallel_dir/$key.status" ]; then
    status=$(cat "$parallel_dir/$key.status")
  else
    status=missing
    echo "== $name"
    echo "-- FAIL: $name (parallel worker ended before reporting)"
  fi
  if [ "$status" != ok ]; then
    failures+=("$name")
  fi
}

start=$(date +%s)
run "cargo build" cargo build --workspace --quiet
run "wasm build" bash scripts/build-wasm.sh
run "docs budget" node scripts/docs-budget.mjs
run "release guard" bash scripts/check-release-guard.sh
run "package typecheck" bun run --cwd packages/snapback4 typecheck
run "package build" bun run --cwd packages/snapback4 build
run "chat app check" bash -c 'cd apps/chat && ../../target/debug/snapback4 check'

(run_logged "cargo-test" "cargo test" node scripts/run-rust-tests.mjs) &

child_pids+=("$!")
(
  run_logged "chat-typecheck" "chat app typecheck" bun run --cwd apps/chat typecheck
  run_logged "expo-typecheck" "expo app typecheck" bun run --cwd apps/chat-expo typecheck

child_pids+=("$!")

for index in "${!child_pids[@]}"; do
  wait "${child_pids[$index]}" || true
  unset 'child_pids[index]'

# @ref LLP 3000#publication-suite-on-small-hosts — a file also owns servers,
# device processes and browser renderers. Budget three available CPUs per slot;
# every client file shares this pool, with no extra shards outside the limit.
# availableParallelism respects affinity; host-wide load does not describe it.
# Rust's compiler and test processes have already drained before clients start.
client_test_cpus=$(node -p 'require("node:os").availableParallelism()')
if [ "${SNAPBACK4_TEST_SERIAL_CLIENT_FILES:-0}" != 1 ]; then
  client_test_concurrency=$((client_test_cpus / 3))
  if [ "$client_test_concurrency" -lt 1 ]; then client_test_concurrency=1; fi
  if [ "$client_test_concurrency" -gt 24 ]; then client_test_concurrency=24; fi
fi
echo "-- available CPUs: $client_test_cpus; client file concurrency: $client_test_concurrency"
(run_logged "client-tests" "client tests" run_client_tests) &
child_pids+=("$!")
wait "${child_pids[0]}" || true
child_pids=()
collect "client-tests" "client tests"
# The two seeded models keep full counts and declared hang guards. Give them a
# serial slot after Rust compilation/tests and every other client file finish.
# The same progress watchdog and single-guard margins apply to model files.
run "client models" node scripts/run-client-tests.mjs 1 models \
  packages/snapback4/test/app-session-model.test.mjs \
  packages/snapback4/test/online-client-model.test.mjs
end=$(date +%s)
echo "== ${#failures[@]} failure(s) in $((end - start)) s"
for f in "${failures[@]:-}"; do [ -n "$f" ] && echo "   $f"; done
[ ${#failures[@]} -eq 0 ]

exec
/bin/bash -lc "sed -n '95,150p' llp/0189-verification-cadence-and-claim-gating.decision.md; cat llp/0232-optimistic-landing-and-main-health.decision.md" in /home/ccheever/projects/sb4-fast
 exited 1 in 0ms:
*complete verification bound to an exact commit* are structurally incompatible
with this repository's landing cadence. Verification per commit must be cheap;
completeness must come from a schedule; claims must be snapshots that
unrelated landings cannot kill.

## Decision

Ruled by the author (Charlie Cheever), 2026-08-07. These are repo-wide
defaults; LLP 0005's honesty rules stay fully in force.

**D-1 — The scheduled complete battery is the authoritative verdict.** The
complete canonical inventory (LLP 0175 D2.3) runs on schedule — at least
twice daily; the current six-hour cadence satisfies this — and on demand.
Its red results become fix-forward filesystem tickets (one per root failure,
idempotent per still-open ticket, following the
`scripts/drift-prone-gates.mjs backstop` pattern) that the fleet fixes
promptly. A battery red does not retroactively invalidate unrelated landings
or standing claims; it names work. Tickets key on the root-failure signature,
not just the gate: each distinct signature gets its own ticket (the filename
carries a stable signature slug alongside the gate), a re-observation of an
unchanged signature updates its existing open ticket, and a new distinct
failure of an already-ticketed gate is never silently coalesced. When a red gate is
claim-relevant to a standing published claim (its D-4 scoped surface), the
ticket names that claim and the claim record names the open ticket while it
stands — "standing, with known red" is a visible state, and it feeds D-3's
triage. A red persisting across four consecutive scheduled runs escalates to
the author.

**D-2 — Landing gates stay fast and affected; frozen-record verdicts are
deferred by default.** Ordinary landings run the LLP 0175 D2.1/D2.2
fast/affected product checks. The pre-push hook still selects and reports the
frozen-record gates affected by the complete ref-update stream, but does not
execute them unless strict mode is explicitly requested. For revisions
targeting `origin/main`, their ordinary verdict moves to the scheduled LLP 0175
drift backstop, whose reds become
visible, attributable fix-forward tickets under D-1/LLP 0232. There is no
ambient same-change frozen-record blocking requirement.

Non-main ref updates are reported but are neither executed nor described as
scheduled in ordinary mode; a later `origin/main` landing is selected from its
own update. The backstop repeats clean content reds under LLP 0232 D-3 before
attribution, records contradictory determinate executions as nondeterminism
without a causal landing, leaves merge topologies that cannot support a
first-parent claim unattributed, and retains runner-setup self-health handling.

If the affected tests passed before integration, the tested semantic delta is
unchanged after merge/rebase, and integration required no semantic conflict
resolution, advancement of `main` alone does not require rerunning expensive
checks. Changed semantic inputs rerun their affected checks; known failing

   226	export function selectChangedPaths(changedFiles, context = loadSelectionContext()) {
   227	  const { policy, calls, inventory } = context;
   228	  validateUniqueStrings(changedFiles, 'changed files');
   229	  const classifications = [];
   230	  const selectedGroups = new Set();
   231	  const matchedRules = new Set();
   232	  const broadFiles = [];
   233	  const unknownFiles = [];
   234	
   235	  for (const path of [...changedFiles].sort()) {
   236	    assert(validRepositoryPath(path), `invalid changed path ${JSON.stringify(path)}`);
   237	    const rule = policy.pathRules.find((candidate) => matchesRule(path, candidate));
   238	    const additiveRules = policy.additivePathRules
   239	      .filter((candidate) => matchesRule(path, candidate));
   240	    const additiveRuleIds = additiveRules.map(({ id }) => id).sort();
   241	    const additiveGroups = additiveRules.flatMap(({ groups }) => groups);
   242	    for (const { id } of additiveRules) matchedRules.add(id);
   243	    for (const group of additiveGroups) selectedGroups.add(group);
   244	    if (!rule) {
   245	      unknownFiles.push(path);
   246	      classifications.push({
   247	        path,
   248	        ruleId: null,
   249	        additiveRuleIds,
   250	        disposition: 'all',
   251	        groups: [...new Set(additiveGroups)].sort(),
   252	      });
   253	      continue;
   254	    }
   255	    matchedRules.add(rule.id);
   256	    const disposition = rule.disposition === 'all' ? 'all' : 'groups';
   257	    const groups = [...new Set([...(rule.groups ?? []), ...additiveGroups])].sort();
   258	    if (disposition === 'all') broadFiles.push(path);
   259	    for (const group of groups) selectedGroups.add(group);
   260	    classifications.push({ path, ruleId: rule.id, additiveRuleIds, disposition, groups });
   261	  }
   262	
   263	  const failBroad = broadFiles.length > 0 || unknownFiles.length > 0;
   264	  if (failBroad) {
   265	    for (const group of inventory.groupNames) selectedGroups.add(group);
   266	  }
   267	  const hasAffectedGroup = [...selectedGroups]
   268	    .some((group) => policy.groups[group].lane === 'affected');
   269	  // Snapback 4 has independent workspaces; legacy Exact/admin is not its baseline.
   270	  if ([...selectedGroups].some(group => group !== 'snapback4' && policy.groups[group].lane === 'affected')) {
   271	    for (const group of policy.affectedBaselineGroups) selectedGroups.add(group);
   272	  }
   273	
   274	  const selected = new Set(policy.alwaysGates);
   275	  for (const group of selectedGroups) {
   276	    for (const gate of policy.groups[group].gates) selected.add(gate);
   277	  }
   278	  closePrerequisites(selected, calls, policy.dependencyOverrides ?? {});
   279	  if (failBroad) for (const gate of inventory.canonicalGates) selected.add(gate);
   280	
   281	  const selectedGates = inventory.canonicalGates.filter((gate) => selected.has(gate));
   282	  const notSelectedGates = inventory.canonicalGates.filter((gate) => !selected.has(gate));
   283	  const selectedSet = new Set(selectedGates);
   284	  const markers = policy.workflowRequirementMarkers;
   285	  const requires = (name) => markers[name].some((gate) => selectedSet.has(gate));
   286	  const selectionClass = failBroad
   287	    ? 'FAIL_CLOSED_FULL'
   288	    : hasAffectedGroup
   289	      ? 'AFFECTED'
   290	      : 'FAST_ONLY';
   291	
   292	  return {
   293	    selectionClass,
   294	    classifications,
   295	    matchedRules: [...matchedRules].sort(),
   296	    selectedGroups: [...selectedGroups].sort(),
   297	    broadFiles,
   298	    unknownFiles,
   299	    selectedGates,
   300	    notSelectedGates,
   301	    requirements: {
   302	      // @ref LLP 0175#d2--assign-every-test-to-an-explicit-cadence — the
   303	      // docs-only core stays dependency-free; fast path-owned gates declare
   304	      // the narrow workflow setup they need instead of widening the core.
   305	      javascriptDependencies: selectionClass !== 'FAST_ONLY'
   306	        || selectedSet.has('generated-artifacts')
   307	        || requires('javascriptDependencies'),
   308	      native: requires('native'),
   309	      rustToolchain: requires('native') || requires('rustToolchain'),
   310	      browser: requires('browser'),
   311	      cargoAudit: requires('cargoAudit'),
   312	      privateRoot: requires('privateRoot'),
   313	    },
   314	  };
   315	}

   318	  changedFiles,
   319	  context = loadSelectionContext(),
   320	  { failClosedDisposition = 'core' } = {},

   381	export function planSelection({
   382	  cadence,
   383	  base = null,
   384	  head = 'HEAD',
   385	  includeWorktree = false,
   386	}) {
   387	  const context = loadSelectionContext();
   388	  const headRevision = resolveCommit(head);
   389	  let requestedBaseRevision = null;
   390	  let baseRevision = null;
   391	  let changedFiles = [];
   392	  let committedChangedFiles = [];
   393	  let worktree = null;
   394	  let selection;
   395	  if (cadence === 'complete') {
   396	    assert(base === null, 'complete cadence does not accept a base');
   397	    assert(includeWorktree === false, 'complete cadence does not accept --include-worktree');
   398	    selection = completeSelection(context);
   399	  } else if (cadence === 'affected') {
   400	    assert(typeof base === 'string' && base.length > 0, 'affected cadence requires a base');
   401	    requestedBaseRevision = resolveCommit(base);
   402	    baseRevision = mergeBase(requestedBaseRevision, headRevision);
   403	    committedChangedFiles = changedPaths(requestedBaseRevision, headRevision);
   404	    // @ref LLP 0175#d25-local-worktree-affected-proof-amendment-2026-08-28 —
   405	    // the union is deduplicated and classified once, so a path present in both
   406	    // the committed diff and the worktree selects exactly like any other path.
   407	    if (includeWorktree) {
   408	      worktree = worktreeSnapshot();
   409	      changedFiles = [...new Set([...committedChangedFiles, ...worktree.paths])].sort();
   410	    } else {
   411	      changedFiles = committedChangedFiles;
   412	    }
   413	    selection = selectChangedPaths(changedFiles, context);
   414	  } else {
   415	    fail(`unsupported cadence ${cadence}`);
   416	  }
   417	  return {
   418	    schemaVersion: 'snapback.local-proof-selection.v1',
   419	    cadence,
   420	    policyDigest: context.policyDigest,
   421	    gateInventoryDigest: context.gateInventoryDigest,
   422	    requestedBaseRevision,
   423	    baseRevision,
   424	    headRevision,
   425	    changedFiles,
   426	    // Committed-only receipts keep their exact v1 shape; the worktree fields
   427	    // appear only when the mode is requested, so commit-bound consumers,
   428	    // replay, CI, hooks, backstops, and claim qualification are untouched.
   429	    worktreeIncluded: includeWorktree,
   430	    ...(includeWorktree ? {
   431	      committedChangedFiles,
   432	      worktreeChangedFiles: worktree.inventory,
   433	      worktreeSelectionDigest: worktree.selectionDigest,
   434	      worktreeContentDigest: worktree.contentDigest,
   435	    } : {}),
   436	    canonicalGateCount: context.inventory.canonicalGates.length,
   437	    selectedGateCount: selection.selectedGates.length,
   438	    selectedGateDigest: digestSelectedGateIds(selection.selectedGates),
   439	    ...selection,
   440	  };
   441	}
   442	
   443	export function selectedGateBytes(gateIds) {
   444	  validateUniqueStrings(gateIds, 'selected gates');
   445	  for (const gateId of gateIds) {
   446	    assert(/^[a-z0-9-]+$/u.test(gateId), `selected gate id ${JSON.stringify(gateId)}`);
   447	  }
   448	  return Buffer.from(gateIds.map((gateId) => `${gateId}\n`).join(''));
   449	}
   450	
   451	export function digestSelectedGateIds(gateIds) {
   452	  return `sha256:${sha256(selectedGateBytes(gateIds))}`;
   453	}
   454	
   455	export function materializeSelectedGates(gateIds, output) {
   456	  assert(typeof output === 'string' && output.length > 0, 'selected gate output path');
   457	  const bytes = selectedGateBytes(gateIds);
   458	  const temporary = resolve(dirname(output), `.selected-gates-${process.pid}-${randomUUID()}.tmp`);
   459	  try {
   460	    writeFileSync(temporary, bytes, { flag: 'wx' });
   461	    renameSync(temporary, output);
   462	  } finally {
   463	    rmSync(temporary, { force: true });
   464	  }
   465	  return {
   466	    selectedGateCount: gateIds.length,
   467	    selectedGateDigest: `sha256:${sha256(bytes)}`,
   468	  };
   469	}
   470	
   471	// @ref LLP 0175#d25-local-worktree-affected-proof-amendment-2026-08-28 —
   472	// recomputed before the outer proof writes a successful completion receipt.
   473	// A selected proof may have been correct for the opening snapshot; it cannot
   474	// claim the closing one. This is completion-receipt invalidation, not a sixth
   475	// gate terminal result, so it returns a verdict rather than failing a gate.
   476	export function recheckWorktreeSnapshot(receipt) {
   477	  return recheckWorktreeSnapshotIn(ROOT, receipt);
   478	}
   479	
   480	export function recheckWorktreeSnapshotIn(root, receipt) {
   481	  if (receipt?.worktreeIncluded !== true) return { checked: false, unchanged: true };
   482	  const current = worktreeSnapshot(root);
   483	  const selectionUnchanged = current.selectionDigest === receipt.worktreeSelectionDigest;
   484	  const contentUnchanged = current.contentDigest === receipt.worktreeContentDigest;
   485	  return {
   486	    checked: true,
   487	    unchanged: selectionUnchanged && contentUnchanged,
   488	    selectionUnchanged,
   489	    contentUnchanged,
   490	    opening: {
   491	      selectionDigest: receipt.worktreeSelectionDigest,
   492	      contentDigest: receipt.worktreeContentDigest,
   493	    },
   494	    closing: {
   495	      selectionDigest: current.selectionDigest,
   496	      contentDigest: current.contentDigest,
   497	    },
   498	  };
   499	}
   500	
   501	export function validateSelectionReceipt(receipt) {
   502	  assert(receipt?.schemaVersion === 'snapback.local-proof-selection.v1', 'selection receipt schema');
   503	  assert(receipt.headRevision === resolveCommit('HEAD'), 'selection receipt matches current HEAD');
   504	  const expected = planSelection({
   505	    cadence: receipt.cadence,
   506	    base: receipt.cadence === 'affected' ? receipt.requestedBaseRevision : null,
   507	    head: receipt.headRevision,
   508	    includeWorktree: receipt.worktreeIncluded === true,
   509	  });
   510	  assert(JSON.stringify(receipt) === JSON.stringify(expected), 'selection receipt is exact and current');
   511	  return receipt;
   512	}
   513	
   514	function main() {
   515	  const [command, ...args] = process.argv.slice(2);
   516	  if (command === 'plan') {
   517	    validateOptionArgs(args, [
   518	      '--cadence', '--output', '--base', '--head', '--github-output',
   519	    ], ['--include-worktree']);
   520	    const cadence = requiredArg(args, '--cadence');
   521	    const output = requiredArg(args, '--output');
   522	    const base = optionalArg(args, '--base');
   523	    const head = optionalArg(args, '--head') ?? 'HEAD';
   524	    const githubOutput = optionalArg(args, '--github-output');
   525	    const receipt = planSelection({
   526	      cadence, base, head, includeWorktree: args.includes('--include-worktree'),
   527	    });
   528	    writeFileSync(output, `${JSON.stringify(receipt, null, 2)}\n`);
   529	    if (githubOutput) writeGitHubOutputs(githubOutput, receipt);
   530	    process.stdout.write(`${JSON.stringify(summary(receipt))}\n`);

repetitions; A 2,262/3,200 and B 1,600 fail. Zero-incremental-CPU sensitivities
remain separate. No policy is accepted for product 3,200/s from this model.

M1b completes 16 cells × three repetitions of independent extended queries,
one Sync/query, depth 1/4/8/16 and NODELAY on/off. Native W2 facts, result
association, error isolation and independent snapshot witnesses pass. Split
frames/NODELAY off reproduce ~41/42 ms p50 A/B; coalescing or NODELAY removes
the stall locally. NODELAY-on depth-4 isolated reads imply optimistic
30-reader ceilings of 148,487/90,869 reads/s, versus depth-1 31,340/20,087;
this is not a loaded 30-reader/admission/SLO qualification. Depth 8/16 on all
30 readers also exceeds 128 external clients. The fixture is M1's 131,072
messages, not S1's 1M seed. 450a's loads 11.55 then 17.01 exclude the optional
cross-host arm; its q24 workload remains untouched.

RFC branches selected: current-layout G2/G3/bounded G3b and plan hygiene;
fill/CPU/service attribution before any A/F write migration; bounded reader
pipelining/admission before conditional G5/G6/D. Warm server work is below
the ≥100 µs/member commissioning trigger. Permanent A/F cancellation still
requires the composed product cycle and G3's real change-evidence insert/prune
tail confirmation. No G9 win follows from harness or model headroom.

The shared-host disk guard stopped epoch 1 at 29.993 GiB, with all owned PIDs
confirmed gone; space later fell to 4.768 GiB after shutdown. Only owned
private data/credentials/unused build cache were removed. After recovery
above 126 GiB and a quiet boundary, a fresh byte-identically configured
cluster completed epoch 2 (99/161 M0 cells per epoch). Thirty foreign-load
attempts, 17 idle-boundary failures, 33 earlier SQL-timing cells and the
closed first epoch remain in external custody. Final normal shutdown,
independent identity/path/port audits and credential cleanup pass; no owned
benchmark PID remains. The independent measurement audit and ref-check
(567 LLPs, 11,862 refs, zero errors) pass. Sources/raw evidence are archived
outside Git with hashes. No raw logs committed; no push, stash or Git config
change.
