## Decision

Ruled by the author (Charlie Cheever), 2026-08-07. These are repo-wide
defaults; LLP 0005's honesty rules stay fully in force.

**D-1 — The scheduled complete battery is the authoritative verdict.** The
complete canonical inventory (LLP 0175 D2.3) runs on schedule — at least
twice daily; the current six-hour cadence satisfies this — and on demand.
Its red results become fix-forward filesystem tickets (one per root failure,
idempotent per still-open ticket, following the
`scripts/drift-prone-gates.mjs backstop` pattern) that the fleet fixes
promptly. A battery red does not retroactively invalidate unrelated landings
or standing claims; it names work. Tickets key on the root-failure signature,
not just the gate: each distinct signature gets its own ticket (the filename
carries a stable signature slug alongside the gate), a re-observation of an
unchanged signature updates its existing open ticket, and a new distinct
failure of an already-ticketed gate is never silently coalesced. When a red gate is
claim-relevant to a standing published claim (its D-4 scoped surface), the
ticket names that claim and the claim record names the open ticket while it
stands — "standing, with known red" is a visible state, and it feeds D-3's
triage. A red persisting across four consecutive scheduled runs escalates to
the author.

**D-2 — Landing gates stay fast and affected; frozen-record verdicts are
deferred by default.** Ordinary landings run the LLP 0175 D2.1/D2.2
fast/affected product checks. The pre-push hook still selects and reports the
frozen-record gates affected by the complete ref-update stream, but does not
execute them unless strict mode is explicitly requested. For revisions
targeting `origin/main`, their ordinary verdict moves to the scheduled LLP 0175
drift backstop, whose reds become
visible, attributable fix-forward tickets under D-1/LLP 0232. There is no
ambient same-change frozen-record blocking requirement.

Non-main ref updates are reported but are neither executed nor described as
scheduled in ordinary mode; a later `origin/main` landing is selected from its
own update. The backstop repeats clean content reds under LLP 0232 D-3 before
attribution, records contradictory determinate executions as nondeterminism
without a causal landing, leaves merge topologies that cannot support a
first-parent claim unattributed, and retains runner-setup self-health handling.

If the affected tests passed before integration, the tested semantic delta is
unchanged after merge/rebase, and integration required no semantic conflict
resolution, advancement of `main` alone does not require rerunning expensive
checks. Changed semantic inputs rerun their affected checks; known failing
affected product tests still require attention. No process, gate, or
qualification loop may require a complete battery at an exact commit as a

     1	//! The guide is compiled. Every ```quarry block in the language card, the
     2	//! keyword cards and the package README binds with no diagnostic and no
     3	//! unexpected note; source-only privacy advice is checked at its declaration;
     4	//! the language card runs against the engine; every refusal code the
     5	//! system emits is one `why` explains; every guide keyword a diagnostic
     6	//! names is a card; the numbers the cards state are the engine's. A card
     7	//! cannot promise a form the parser refuses (LLP 3000 §7, 2026-09-13).
     8

    23	const README: &str = concat!(include_str!("../../../packages/snapback4/README.md"), "\n", include_str!("../../../packages/snapback4/CLIENT-AND-OPERATIONS.md"), "\n", include_str!("../../../packages/snapback4/SERVER.md"));
    24	
    25	// A relocated assertion checks the actual orientation door as well as its destination.
    26	fn readme_reference(name: &str) -> &'static str {
    27	    let map = include_str!("../../../packages/snapback4/README.md").split_once("## Reference map").unwrap().1;
    28	    assert!(map.contains(&format!("`guide {name}`")), "orientation omits guide {name}");
    29	    card(name).unwrap()
    30	}
    31	
    32	#[test]
    33	fn feeds_card_states_background_contract_and_keeps_recipe_refusals() {
    34	    let feeds = card("feeds").unwrap();
    35	    assert_eq!(feeds, readme_reference("feeds"));
    36	    let contract = feeds.split_once("## Following contract").unwrap().1.split_once("## Boundaries").unwrap().0
    37	        .split_whitespace().collect::<Vec<_>>().join(" ");
    38	    for fact in ["newest first", "Your own events appear immediately", "normally within a second", "copies their newest 50 live events immediately, then the rest of a 1,000-event window", "Older posts remain in `posts` and are not copied.", "Unfollow hides that actor on the next read", "your backfill or cleanup is pending", "Removal hides the event immediately", "without prediction"] {
    39	        assert!(contract.contains(fact), "missing {fact}");
    40	    }
    41	    for bad in ["skips a follower", "skip saturated readers", "A skipped follower", "not skipped\nauthors"] {
    42	        assert!(!feeds.contains(bad), "{bad}");
    43	    }
    44	    for code in ["FEED_PULL_CAP", "FEED_PROMOTION_PULL_CAP", "E_RECIPE_RETIRED"] {
    45	        assert_eq!(snapback4_lang::diag::guide_for(code), "feeds");
    46	        assert!(explain_code(code).unwrap().contains("snapback4 guide feeds"));
    47	        assert!(codes().contains(&code));
    48	    }
    49	    for (code, facts) in [
    50	        ("E_RECIPE_RETIRED", vec!["view following: activity", "the engine chooses delivery bounds"]),
    51	        ("E_TIMELINE_FILTER", vec!["view activity", "posts where .parentId = null and not .removed"]),
    52	        ("E_TIMELINE_READ", vec!["following last 20 by .at after c"]),
    53	        ("E_TIMELINE_ACTIVATE", vec!["query home(c: cursor ?): return activity where .actor = viewer or exists follows[viewer, .actor] last 20 by .at after c", "Original event ids are post ids", "--allow-feed-adoption", "5,000,000 copies"]),
    54	        ("FEED_PULL_CAP", vec!["32 shared authors", "rolled back", "new write ID"]),
    55	        ("FEED_PROMOTION_PULL_CAP", vec!["32 shared-author slots", "rolled back", "public", "pullCounts", "server-run query", "used = 32"]),
    56	    ] {
    57	        let explanation = explain_code(code).unwrap();
    58	        for fact in facts { assert!(explanation.contains(fact), "{code}: missing {fact}"); }
    59	        if ["E_RECIPE_RETIRED", "E_TIMELINE_FILTER", "E_TIMELINE_READ"].contains(&code) {
    60	            for old in ["maintain feed: timeline", "events posts where", "NAME[viewer]"] {
    61	                assert!(!explanation.contains(old), "{code}: why still teaches {old}");
    62	            }
    63	        }
    64	    }
    65	    for retired in ["E_RECIPE_BRIDGE", "E_RECIPE_CAPS"] { assert!(!codes().contains(&retired)); }
    66	}

    68	#[test]
    69	fn w11r1c_residency_fact_cards_require_verbose() {
    70	    for name in ["residency", "sync", "services"] {
    71	        let text = card(name).unwrap();
    72	        assert!(text.contains("check --residency --verbose"), "{name}");
    73	        if name == "sync" { assert!(text.contains("`check --residency` names the proven")); }
    74	        else { assert!(!text.contains("`check --residency`"), "{name}: bare command promises facts"); }
    75	    }
    76	}

snapback4/packages/snapback4/test/web-start.test.mjs:41:const require = createRequire(join(root, 'apps/chat/package.json'));
11:export function lintPackagedDocs(root) {
// @ref LLP 3000.007#20.1 — published discovery text must describe the product.
import { readFileSync, readdirSync } from 'node:fs';
import { join } from 'node:path';

const ownership = /\b(?:RULES\d+|KIT\d+|SURF\d+|LANG\d+|DAY\d+)\b|\bREG\s+owns\b|\b[Ww]ave[- ]?\d+\b|\b(?:DX\d*|HAND\d*|NARR\d*|PROOF\d*|QDIAG\d*|PAGE\d*|EX\d+)\s+owns\b|\bin (?:its|the) workstream\b/g;
export function docLeaks(text) {
  return Array.from(text.matchAll(ownership), match => ({
    line: text.slice(0, match.index).split('\n').length, text: match[0],
  }));
}
export function lintPackagedDocs(root) {
  const files = [];
  function walk(dir, nested) {
    for (const entry of readdirSync(dir, { withFileTypes: true })) {
      const path = join(dir, entry.name);
      if (entry.isDirectory() && nested) walk(path, true);
      else if (entry.isFile() && /\.(?:md|rs)$/.test(entry.name)) files.push(path);
    }
  }
  walk(join(root, 'packages/snapback4'), false);
  walk(join(root, 'crates/snapback4-lang/src/guide'), true);
  files.push(join(root, 'crates/snapback4-lang/src/guide.rs'));
  const leaks = files.flatMap(path => docLeaks(readFileSync(path, 'utf8')).map(hit => `${path}:${hit.line}: ${hit.text}`));
  if (leaks.length) throw new Error(`packaged docs contain workstream ownership text:\n${leaks.join('\n')}`);
}
// W14b revision 2 §6: count physical lines and require the in-file first-screen path.
import { readFileSync } from "node:fs";
import { spawnSync } from "node:child_process";
import { fileURLToPath } from "node:url";

const root = new URL("../", import.meta.url);
const budget = JSON.parse(readFileSync(new URL("metrics/docs-budget.json", root), "utf8"));
const readme = readFileSync(new URL("packages/snapback4/README.md", root), "utf8");
const actual = (readme.match(/\n/g)?.length ?? 0) + Number(readme.length > 0 && !readme.endsWith("\n"));
const valid = Number.isSafeInteger(budget.readmeLines) && budget.readmeLines > 0 &&
  typeof budget.set === "string" && budget.set.length > 0 && typeof budget.why === "string" && budget.why.length > 0;
console.log(`docs budget: README ${actual}/${budget.readmeLines} physical lines`);
if (!valid) console.error("docs budget: expected a positive readmeLines budget, set date and reason");
if (actual > budget.readmeLines) console.error("docs budget: README exceeds its budget; preserve the first-screen path when editing");
// Run even after a size failure: the suite reports both failures, and size alone cannot qualify the orientation.
const sufficient = spawnSync("cargo", ["test", "-p", "snapback4-lang", "--test", "guide", "w14b_first_account_screen_is_self_contained", "--", "--exact"],
  { cwd: fileURLToPath(root), encoding: "utf8" });
process.stdout.write(sufficient.stdout ?? "");
process.stderr.write(sufficient.stderr ?? "");
const ran = /test result: ok\. 1 passed; 0 failed;/.test(sufficient.stdout ?? "");
if (sufficient.status === 0 && !ran) console.error("docs budget: the named self-containment test must run exactly once");
if (sufficient.error) console.error(`docs budget: self-containment test could not run: ${sufficient.error.message}`);
process.exitCode = valid && actual <= budget.readmeLines && sufficient.status === 0 && ran ? 0 : 1;

   378	
   379	## Snapback 4 affected selection (2026-10-24)
   380	
   381	Author direction: Workstream FAST, Charlie Cheever, 2026-10-24. This implements
   382	D-2 for the independent Snapback 4 workspace. It does not amend D-1, D-6, LLP
   383	0232's optimistic integration rule, or any publication protocol.
   384	
   385	`node snapback4/scripts/affected.mjs --base REV [--head REV | --worktree]
   386	[--json] [--run]` selects the merge-base-to-head Git diff. Worktree mode adds
   387	staged, unstaged, deleted, rename endpoints and non-ignored untracked paths.
   388	Historical planning reads the selected revision's manifests and test sources;
   389	execution requires that revision checked out. The root local-proof policy
   390	selects a `snapback4-affected` delegation gate for `snapback4/` paths. This
   391	independent workspace does not inherit the legacy Exact/admin baseline;
   392	other root groups and root unknown-path fallback retain it.
   393	
   394	Selection ownership:
   395	
   396	- Cargo metadata supplies workspace membership, optional/dev dependency edges
   397	  and integration targets. Source changes select the package and its transitive
   398	  reverse dependencies. A test-only file selects its exact integration target;
   399	  shared/nested support widens instead. Required target features are included.
   400	- Package manifests supply client dependency edges, including peer/dev edges.
   401	  Source changes select the package's test pool and its reverse dependencies;
   402	  a test-only diff selects named files. Tests' literal imports/re-exports,
   403	  dynamic literal imports, resource URLs and helpers are followed to find CLI
   404	  process and wasm consumers. Unresolved local/computed imports conservatively
   405	  count as both consumers. Tests spawning processes are conservatively CLI
   406	  consumers. Selected clients rebuild the products they consume. Existing
   407	  process-custody pooling and serial model custody are reused.
   408	- Guide/docs select check-guide, docs budget and packaged-docs lint; Snapback 4
   409	  guide cards also select the compiled guide target. Rust source movement
   410	  conservatively selects diagnostics-registry checking and check-inventories,
   411	  even without a new code. Language/generated inputs select generated-artifact
   412	  checking; affected references and LLPs select ref-check. Device reverse
   413	  dependents select wasm measurement. Language/core semantics select Lean
   414	  build, difftest run-lean and a seeded 2,000-case run.
   415	- Unknown paths, locks, workspace/package manifests, toolchains, build scripts,
   416	  proc-macros, shared test support and vendored inputs select all workspace
   417	  packages, client pools and cross-cutting checks. Each triggering file has a
   418	  reason. A package with no owned test runner also widens, covering tests held
   419	  in sibling packages. Selector implementation/fixture edits select their own
   420	  tests rather than product pools. Unowned root paths remain broad.
   421	
   422	Every execution command has a finite timeout and inherits
   423	`CARGO_INCREMENTAL=0 CARGO_PROFILE_DEV_DEBUG=0 CARGO_PROFILE_TEST_DEBUG=0`.
   424	Cargo disables target-level fail-fast; independent commands continue after
   425	failures, including producer failures (which remain visible). A JSON receipt
   426	under `target/affected/` records base/head, changed paths, selected commands,
   427	per-file reasons and per-command status/timing. It records incomplete execution
   428	before starting and refuses success if the observed Git/content snapshot changes.
   429	An affected receipt is landing evidence, never a packed-publication receipt.
   430	
   431	A historical red HEAD does not mean its last commit introduced that red. The
   432	maintained-column regression at `cadb7c55be` must be selected from the range
   433	starting before its originating change `202826334f`; selecting only the
   434	last documentation commit cannot discover inherited product failures. The
   435	regression fixture requires core/lang packages and residency.test.mjs for that
   436	originating range. Scheduled full-battery results remain the failure oracle;
   437	selection replay alone is not execution or a no-missed-failures proof.

     1	#!/usr/bin/env bash
     2	# The device as wasm for the web (LLP 3000 §7, one implementation): the
     3	# size-first profile, wasm-bindgen's web target, the locked Binaryen optimizer,
     4	# into packages/snapback4/src/wasm/ — built, never committed; the package
     5	# build carries it into dist/wasm/. `scripts/measure-wasm.sh` measures the same build.
     6	set -euo pipefail
     7	cd "$(dirname "$0")/.."
     8	node scripts/wasm-budget.mjs >/dev/null
     9	# @ref LLP 3000.002#proposed-device-representation-and-codec-redesign — publication hosts use the same portable, pinned optimizer; PATH is irrelevant.
    10	optimizer=node_modules/binaryen/bin/wasm-opt
    11	if [ ! -f "$optimizer" ]; then
    12	  echo "wasm build: missing locked Binaryen; run bun install --frozen-lockfile in snapback4/" >&2
    13	  exit 1
    14	fi
    15	node -e 'const wanted = require("./package.json").devDependencies.binaryen;
    16	const installed = require("./node_modules/binaryen/package.json").version;
    17	if (!/^\d+\.\d+\.\d+$/.test(wanted) || installed !== wanted) {
    18	  throw new Error("wasm build: Binaryen differs from the exact workspace pin; run bun install --frozen-lockfile");
    19	}
    20	console.log(`Binaryen ${installed} (locked workspace dependency)`);'
    21	out=packages/snapback4/src/wasm
    22	CARGO_INCREMENTAL=0 CARGO_PROFILE_DEV_DEBUG=0 CARGO_PROFILE_TEST_DEBUG=0 cargo build -p snapback4-device --no-default-features --features web --profile wasm --target wasm32-unknown-unknown --quiet
    23	rm -rf "$out"
    24	wasm-bindgen --target web --out-dir "$out" --out-name snapback4_device target/wasm32-unknown-unknown/wasm/snapback4_device.wasm
    25	wasm=$out/snapback4_device_bg.wasm
    26	node "$optimizer" -Os --enable-bulk-memory --enable-nontrapping-float-to-int -o "$out/os.wasm" "$wasm"
    27	if [ "$(gzip -9 -c < "$out/os.wasm" | wc -c)" -lt "$(gzip -9 -c < "$wasm" | wc -c)" ]; then mv "$out/os.wasm" "$wasm"; else rm "$out/os.wasm"; fi
    28	# @ref LLP 3000.002#proposed-device-representation-and-codec-redesign — preserve Rust's size choices while sharing identical branch tails (also measured by HV).
    29	# Compare identical gzip headers; temporary filenames must not buy bytes.
    30	node "$optimizer" --code-folding --duplicate-function-elimination --dce --vacuum --enable-bulk-memory --enable-nontrapping-float-to-int -o "$out/fold.wasm" "$wasm"
    31	if [ "$(gzip -9 -c < "$out/fold.wasm" | wc -c)" -lt "$(gzip -9 -c < "$wasm" | wc -c)" ]; then mv "$out/fold.wasm" "$wasm"; else rm "$out/fold.wasm"; fi
    32	# ES2022 consumers use free(); bindgen's optional ESNext disposal is omitted.
    33	node -e 'const fs = require("node:fs"), path = process.argv[1];
    34	fs.writeFileSync(path, fs.readFileSync(path, "utf8").replace(/^\s*\[Symbol\.dispose\]\(\): void;\n/gm, ""));' "$out/snapback4_device.d.ts"
    35	rm -f "$out/.gitignore" "$out/package.json"
    36	node scripts/stamp-device.mjs "$wasm"
    37	gzip_bytes=$(gzip -9 -c "$wasm" | wc -c | tr -d ' ')
    38	status=$(node scripts/wasm-budget.mjs metrics/wasm-budget.json "$gzip_bytes")
    39	printf 'wasm %s B raw, %s B gzip %s\n' "$(wc -c < "$wasm" | tr -d ' ')" "$gzip_bytes" "$status"
    40	# @ref LLP 3000.002#proposed-device-representation-and-codec-redesign — the budget is a ratchet (author ruling 2026-09-18): an overrun fails the build step.
    41	case "$status" in *OVER*)
    42	  echo "wasm budget: the web device exceeds metrics/wasm-budget.json. A landing that needs the bytes raises the budget in the same commit, with the reason in its why, the commit message and the journal (LLP 3000.002; issues/20260913-snapback4-wasm-device-diet.md)." >&2
    43	  exit 1;;
    44	esac
     1	#!/usr/bin/env bash
     2	# The device core as wasm, measured on the pinned toolchain: the size-first
     3	# profile, wasm-bindgen's web target, gzip-9 and brotli-11, one line to
     4	# metrics/wasm.jsonl (LLP 3000 §7: one implementation; the web ships this).

    10	if [ -n "$source_status" ]; then
    11	  dirty=true
    12	  echo 'WARNING: measuring a dirty source tree; sha identifies the base, not the measured artifact (dirty=true).' >&2
    13	fi
    14	node scripts/attribute-wasm.mjs --check-families --check-absent >&2
    15	# @ref LLP 3000.002#proposed-device-representation-and-codec-redesign — publication hosts use the same portable, pinned optimizer; PATH is irrelevant.
    16	optimizer=node_modules/binaryen/bin/wasm-opt
    17	if [ ! -f "$optimizer" ]; then

    19	  exit 1

    25	}

    30	wasm=$out/snapback4_device_bg.wasm
    31	glue=$out/snapback4_device.js
    32	optimized=false
    33	node "$optimizer" -Os --enable-bulk-memory --enable-nontrapping-float-to-int -o "$out/os.wasm" "$wasm"
    34	if [ "$(gzip -9 -c < "$out/os.wasm" | wc -c)" -lt "$(gzip -9 -c < "$wasm" | wc -c)" ]; then optimized=true; mv "$out/os.wasm" "$wasm"; else rm "$out/os.wasm"; fi
    35	# @ref LLP 3000.002#proposed-device-representation-and-codec-redesign — preserve Rust's size choices while sharing identical branch tails (also measured by HV).
    36	# Compare identical gzip headers; temporary filenames must not buy bytes.
    37	node "$optimizer" --code-folding --duplicate-function-elimination --dce --vacuum --enable-bulk-memory --enable-nontrapping-float-to-int -o "$out/fold.wasm" "$wasm"
    38	if [ "$(gzip -9 -c < "$out/fold.wasm" | wc -c)" -lt "$(gzip -9 -c < "$wasm" | wc -c)" ]; then optimized=true; mv "$out/fold.wasm" "$wasm"; else rm "$out/fold.wasm"; fi
    39	node scripts/stamp-device.mjs "$wasm"
    40	sizes() { printf '{"raw":%d,"gzip":%d,"brotli":%d}' "$(wc -c < "$1")" "$(gzip -9 -c "$1" | wc -c)" "$(brotli -c -q 11 "$1" | wc -c)"; }
    41	over=$(( $(gzip -9 -c "$wasm" | wc -c) - budget ))
    42	if [ "$over" -lt 0 ]; then over=0; fi
    43	line=$(printf '{"at":"%s","sha":"%s","dirty":%s,"wasm":%s,"glue":%s,"wasmOpt":%s,"budget":%d,"over":%d}' "$(date -u +%Y-%m-%dT%H:%M:%SZ)" "$(git rev-parse --short HEAD)" "$dirty" "$(sizes "$wasm")" "$(sizes "$glue")" "$optimized" "$budget" "$over")
    44	echo "$line" | tee -a metrics/wasm.jsonl
    45	rm -rf "$out"

          rustup toolchain install

snapback4/scripts/affected.test.mjs 2026-10-24 11:24:38.800819453 +0200
#!/usr/bin/env node
// Replay selection only: no test-execution or historical-green claim.
import { execFileSync } from 'node:child_process';
import { writeFileSync } from 'node:fs';
import { resolve } from 'node:path';
import { fileURLToPath } from 'node:url';
import { CARGO_ENV, ROOT, changedPaths, modelAtRevision, selectAffected } from './affected.mjs';

export function obligations(plan, model) {
  const selected = new Set();
  for (const crate of model.crates) {
    if (plan.rustPackages.includes(crate.name)) {
      for (const target of crate.targets) if (target.kind.some(kind => ['lib', 'bin', 'test', 'proc-macro'].includes(kind))) selected.add(`rust:${crate.name}:${target.kind.join('+')}:${target.name}`);
      if (crate.targets.some(target => target.kind.includes('lib'))) selected.add(`rust:${crate.name}:doc`);
    } else for (const target of plan.rustTargets[crate.name] ?? []) selected.add(`rust:${crate.name}:test:${target}`);
  }
  for (const file of plan.clientFiles) selected.add(`client:${file}`);
  for (const check of plan.checks) selected.add(`check:${check}`);
  for (const step of plan.steps) if (step.id.startsWith('difftest:')) selected.add(step.id);
  return [...selected].sort();
}
export function replay({ ref = 'origin/main', count = 30 } = {}) {
  const git = (...args) => execFileSync('timeout', ['120s', 'git', ...args], { cwd: ROOT, env: { ...process.env, ...CARGO_ENV }, encoding: 'utf8' }).trim();
  const commits = git('log', ref, '--merges', `-n${count}`, '--format=%H', '--', 'snapback4/').split('\n').filter(Boolean);
  const rows = commits.map((head, index) => {
    const base = git('rev-parse', `${head}^1`);
    const files = changedPaths(ROOT, base, head);
    const started = Date.now();
    return modelAtRevision(ROOT, head, model => {
      const plan = selectAffected(files, model);
      const full = selectAffected(['snapback4/Cargo.toml'], model);
      const selectedSet = obligations(plan, model);
      const fullSet = obligations(full, model);
      const result = { base, head, subject: git('show', '-s', '--format=%s', head), files, broad: plan.broad,
        selectedCount: selectedSet.length, fullCount: fullSet.length, ratio: selectedSet.length / fullSet.length,
        selectedCommandCount: plan.steps.length, fullCommandCount: full.steps.length,
        rustPackages: plan.rustPackages, rustTargets: plan.rustTargets, clientFiles: plan.clientFiles,
        reasons: plan.reasons, selectedSet, elapsedMs: Date.now() - started };
      console.error(`${index + 1}/${commits.length} ${head.slice(0, 10)}: ${result.selectedCount}/${result.fullCount} obligations; ${result.selectedCommandCount}/${result.fullCommandCount} commands; broad=${plan.broad}`);
      return result;
    });
  });
  return { schemaVersion: 'snapback4.affected-replay.v1', ref, refRevision: git('rev-parse', ref), requestedCount: count,
    measurement: 'Selection only; Rust target/client-file/check obligations, not individual test counts or timing.', rows };
}
if (process.argv[1] && resolve(process.argv[1]) === fileURLToPath(import.meta.url)) {
  const args = process.argv.slice(2); const options = {}; let output;
  for (let i = 0; i < args.length; i++) {
    if (!['--ref', '--count', '--output'].includes(args[i]) || !args[i + 1]) throw new Error('usage: affected-replay.mjs [--ref REV] [--count N] [--output PATH]');
    const key = args[i].slice(2); const value = args[++i];
    if (key === 'output') output = value; else options[key] = key === 'count' ? Number(value) : value;
  }
  if (options.count !== undefined && (!Number.isInteger(options.count) || options.count < 1)) throw new Error('positive integer count required');
  const result = replay(options); const bytes = JSON.stringify(result, null, 2) + '\n';
  if (output) writeFileSync(output, bytes); else console.log(bytes);
}

     1	#!/usr/bin/env bash
     2	# `web-device-check` gives debug test witnesses the web device's policy on a
     3	# native build. The published artifacts build under `production` and `wasm`,
     4	# which leave debug assertions off, and the core crate refuses the feature
     5	# there. This builds the web artifact's exact configuration with the feature
     6	# added and requires that refusal, so deleting the guard turns the suite red.
     7	# A hand-made profile that turns debug assertions on is outside this promise.
     8	# The same gate checks raw fixture access against the host's release profile.
     9	set -u
    10	cd "$(dirname "$0")/.."
    11	fixture_status=0
    12	bash scripts/check-test-support-guard.sh || fixture_status=1
    13	out=$(cargo check -p snapback4-device --no-default-features --features web,web-device-check --profile wasm --target wasm32-unknown-unknown --quiet 2>&1)
    14	status=$?
    15	if [ "$status" = 0 ]; then
    16	  echo "release guard: web-device-check compiled under the wasm profile; the refusal in snapback4-core/src/lib.rs is gone"
    17	  exit 1
    18	fi
    19	if ! printf '%s\n' "$out" | grep -q 'web-device-check is only for debug test witnesses'; then
    20	  printf '%s\n' "$out"
    21	  echo "release guard: the build failed for another reason (exit $status), so the refusal was not observed"
    22	  exit 1
    23	fi
    24	echo "release guard: ok (web-device-check refuses the wasm profile)"
    25	exit "$fixture_status"
     1	#!/usr/bin/env node
     2	// Build the complete Cargo test inventory once, then run its independent test
     3	// binaries concurrently. `cargo test --workspace` serializes those binaries,
     4	// while starting one Cargo frontend per target makes them contend for Cargo's
     5	// build lock. The artifact stream is Cargo's authoritative inventory, so a new
     6	// target is included automatically rather than maintained in a parallel list.
     7	
     8	import { availableParallelism } from "node:os";
     9	import { spawn } from "node:child_process";
    10	
    11	const requested = Number.parseInt(process.env.SNAPBACK4_RUST_TEST_PROCESSES ?? "", 10);
    12	const processLimit = Number.isFinite(requested) && requested > 0
    13	  ? requested
    14	  : Math.min(24, Math.max(4, availableParallelism()));
    15	const active = new Set();
    16	let interrupted = null;
    17	
    18	for (const signal of ["SIGINT", "SIGTERM"]) {
    19	  process.on(signal, () => {
    20	    interrupted ??= signal === "SIGINT" ? 130 : 143;
    21	    for (const child of active) child.kill(signal);
    22	  });
    23	}
    24	
    25	function run(command, args, options = {}) {
    26	  return new Promise((resolve) => {
    27	    const child = spawn(command, args, options);
    28	    active.add(child);
    29	    child.on("error", (error) => resolve({ code: 1, error }));
    30	    child.on("close", (code, signal) => {
    31	      active.delete(child);
    32	      resolve({ code: code ?? (signal ? 1 : 0), signal });
    33	    });
    34	  });
    35	}
    36	
    37	async function cargoArtifacts() {
    38	  const artifacts = [];
    39	  const child = spawn("cargo", ["test", "--workspace", "--no-run", "--message-format=json-render-diagnostics"], {
    40	    stdio: ["ignore", "pipe", "pipe"],
    41	  });
    42	  active.add(child);
    43	  child.stderr.pipe(process.stderr);
    44	  let pending = "";
    45	  child.stdout.setEncoding("utf8");
    46	  child.stdout.on("data", (chunk) => {
    47	    pending += chunk;
    48	    const lines = pending.split("\n");
    49	    pending = lines.pop() ?? "";
    50	    for (const line of lines) inspectArtifactLine(line, artifacts);
    51	  });
    52	  const result = await new Promise((resolve) => {
    53	    child.on("error", (error) => resolve({ code: 1, error }));
    54	    child.on("close", (code, signal) => {
    55	      active.delete(child);
    56	      resolve({ code: code ?? (signal ? 1 : 0), signal });
    57	    });
    58	  });
    59	  if (pending) inspectArtifactLine(pending, artifacts);
    60	  return { ...result, artifacts: [...new Map(artifacts.map((artifact) => [artifact.executable, artifact])).values()] };
    61	}
    62	
    63	function inspectArtifactLine(line, artifacts) {
    64	  try {
    65	    const message = JSON.parse(line);
    66	    if (message.reason === "compiler-message" && message.message?.rendered) {
    67	      process.stderr.write(message.message.rendered);
    68	    }
    69	    if (message.reason === "compiler-artifact" && message.executable && message.profile?.test) {
    70	      artifacts.push({
    71	        executable: message.executable,
    72	        packageId: message.package_id,
    73	        target: message.target.name,
    74	      });
    75	    }
    76	  } catch {
    77	    if (line) process.stdout.write(`${line}\n`);
    78	  }
    79	}
    80	
    81	function packageName(packageId) {
    82	  const pathMatch = packageId.match(/\/crates\/([^/#]+)#/);
    83	  return pathMatch?.[1] ?? packageId.split(" ")[0];
    84	}
    85	
    86	function testThreads(artifact) {
    87	  const packageNameValue = packageName(artifact.packageId);
    88	  if (packageNameValue === "snapback4-sim") return 4;
    89	  if (packageNameValue === "snapback4-device") return artifact.target === "device" ? 8 : 2;
    90	  if (packageNameValue === "snapback4") return ["lifecycle", "snapback4", "witness"].includes(artifact.target) ? 1 : 2;
    91	  if (packageNameValue === "snapback4-lang" && artifact.target === "lowering") return 8;
    92	  return 4;
    93	}
    94	
    95	async function listedTests(artifact) {
    96	  const chunks = [];
    97	  const errors = [];
    98	  const child = spawn(artifact.executable, ["--list", "--format=terse"], {
    99	    env: process.env,
   100	    stdio: ["ignore", "pipe", "pipe"],
   101	  });
   102	  active.add(child);
   103	  child.stdout.on("data", (chunk) => chunks.push(chunk));
   104	  child.stderr.on("data", (chunk) => errors.push(chunk));
   105	  const result = await new Promise((resolve) => {
   106	    child.on("error", (error) => resolve({ code: 1, error }));
   107	    child.on("close", (code, signal) => {
   108	      active.delete(child);
   109	      resolve({ code: code ?? (signal ? 1 : 0), signal });
   110	    });
   111	  });
   112	  if (result.code !== 0) {
   113	    throw new Error(`could not list ${packageName(artifact.packageId)}::${artifact.target}: ${Buffer.concat(errors).toString("utf8")}`);
   114	  }
   115	  return Buffer.concat(chunks).toString("utf8").split("\n").filter((line) => line.endsWith(": test")).map((line) => line.slice(0, -6));
   116	}
   117	
   118	async function runArtifact(artifact) {
   119	  const label = `${packageName(artifact.packageId)}::${artifact.target}${artifact.labelSuffix ?? ""}`;
   120	  const started = Date.now();
   121	  const chunks = [];
   122	  const env = { ...process.env };
   123	  if (packageName(artifact.packageId) === "snapback4-device") env.RUST_MIN_STACK = "33554432";
   124	  const args = ["--quiet", `--test-threads=${testThreads(artifact)}`];
   125	  if (artifact.filter) args.push(artifact.filter);
   126	  if (artifact.exact) args.push("--exact");
   127	  const child = spawn(artifact.executable, args, {
   128	    env,
   129	    stdio: ["ignore", "pipe", "pipe"],
   130	  });
   131	  active.add(child);
   132	  child.stdout.on("data", (chunk) => chunks.push(chunk));
   133	  child.stderr.on("data", (chunk) => chunks.push(chunk));
   134	  const result = await new Promise((resolve) => {
   135	    child.on("error", (error) => resolve({ code: 1, error }));
   136	    child.on("close", (code, signal) => {
   137	      active.delete(child);
   138	      resolve({ code: code ?? (signal ? 1 : 0), signal });
   139	    });
   140	  });
   141	  const output = Buffer.concat(chunks).toString("utf8");
   142	  const summary = [...output.matchAll(/test result: [^\n]+/g)].at(-1)?.[0] ?? "no test summary";
   143	  const elapsed = ((Date.now() - started) / 1000).toFixed(1);
   144	  if (result.code === 0) {
   145	    console.log(`-- rust target: ${label} (${elapsed} s): ${summary}`);
   146	  } else {
   147	    console.error(`-- FAIL rust target: ${label} (${elapsed} s)`);
   148	    process.stderr.write(output);
   149	  }
   150	  return { ...result, label };

    32	function checkReferences(rows) {
    33	  const users = new Set(rows.users.map(row => row.id));
    34	  const posts = new Set([...rows.posts, ...rows.replies].map(row => row.id));
    35	  const conversations = new Set(rows.conversations.map(row => row.id));
    36	  const messages = new Map(rows.messages.map(row => [row.id, row]));
    37	  const members = new Set(rows.memberships.map(row => `${row.conversationId}:${row.userId}`));
    38	  for (const [table, entries] of Object.entries(rows)) for (const row of entries) {
    39	    for (const key of ['userId', 'authorId', 'senderId', 'creatorId', 'targetUserId']) {
    40	      if (row[key]) assert.ok(users.has(row[key]), `${table}.${row.id}.${key}`);
    41	    }
    42	    if (row.conversationId) assert.ok(conversations.has(row.conversationId));
    43	    if (row.postId) assert.ok(posts.has(row.postId));
    44	    if (row.parentPostId) assert.ok(posts.has(row.parentPostId));
    45	    if (row.messageId) assert.ok(messages.has(row.messageId));
    46	    if (table === 'profiles') assert.ok(users.has(row.id));
    47	    if (table === 'messages') {
    48	      assert.ok(members.has(`${row.conversationId}:${row.senderId}`));
    49	      if (row.quoteId) assert.equal(messages.get(row.quoteId)?.conversationId, row.conversationId);
    50	      if (row.media) {
    51	        assert.deepEqual(Object.keys(row.media).sort(), ['byteSize', 'path']);
    52	        assert.equal(row.media.byteSize, statSync(new URL(row.media.path, fixtureRoot)).size);
    53	      }
    54	    }
    55	  }

   240	export function changedPaths(root, base, head, worktree = false) {
   241	  const committed = paths(git(root, 'diff', '--name-only', '-z', '--no-renames', `${base}...${head}`));
   242	  return sorted([...committed, ...(worktree ? [
   243	    ...paths(git(root, 'diff', '--name-only', '-z', '--no-renames', 'HEAD')),
   244	    ...paths(git(root, 'ls-files', '--others', '--exclude-standard', '-z')),
   245	  ] : [])]);
   246	}
   247	function snapshotDigest(root, files) {
   248	  return hash(JSON.stringify(files.map(path => [path, existsSync(join(root, path)) ? hash(readFileSync(join(root, path))) : null])));
   249	}
   250	
   251	// Historical planning reads the selected revision's Cargo graph and test sources.
   252	// The scratch snapshot is confined to this worktree's target and never runs tests.
   253	export function modelAtRevision(root, revision, fn) {
   254	  const parent = join(root, 'target/affected'); mkdirSync(parent, { recursive: true });
   255	  const snapshot = mkdtempSync(join(parent, 'snapshot-'));
   256	  try {
   257	    const tree = git(root, 'ls-tree', '-r', '--name-only', revision, '--', 'snapback4').trim().split('\n');
   258	    const wanted = ['snapback4/Cargo.toml', 'snapback4/Cargo.lock', 'snapback4/package.json', 'snapback4/crates', 'snapback4/packages', 'snapback4/scripts'];
   259	    const present = wanted.filter(path => tree.some(file => file === path || file.startsWith(path + '/')));
   260	    const archive = command(root, 'git', ['archive', revision, '--', ...present], { encoding: null });
   261	    command(snapshot, 'tar', ['-xf', '-'], { input: archive });
   262	    return fn(loadModel(snapshot));
   263	  } finally { rmSync(snapshot, { recursive: true, force: true }); }
   264	}
   265	export function planAffected({ root = ROOT, base = 'origin/main', head = 'HEAD', worktree = false, files } = {}) {
   266	  const headRevision = git(root, 'rev-parse', '--verify', `${head}^{commit}`).trim();
   267	  const baseRevision = git(root, 'rev-parse', '--verify', `${base}^{commit}`).trim();
   268	  const observedFiles = changedPaths(root, baseRevision, headRevision, worktree);
   269	  const changed = files ?? observedFiles;
   270	  const current = git(root, 'rev-parse', 'HEAD').trim();
   271	  if (worktree && headRevision !== current) throw new Error('--worktree requires current HEAD');
   272	  const select = model => selectAffected(changed, model);
   273	  const plan = worktree ? select(loadModel(root)) : modelAtRevision(root, headRevision, select);
   274	  return { ...plan, base: baseRevision, mergeBase: git(root, 'merge-base', baseRevision, headRevision).trim(), head: headRevision,
   275	    worktree, observedFiles, inputDigest: snapshotDigest(root, observedFiles) };
   276	}
   277	const quote = value => `'${value.replaceAll("'", "'\\''")}'`;
   278	export function formatStep(step) {
   279	  return `(cd ${quote(step.cwd)} && timeout ${step.timeoutSeconds}s env ${Object.entries(CARGO_ENV).map(([key, value]) => `${key}=${value}`).join(' ')} ${step.argv.map(quote).join(' ')})`;
   280	}
   281	export async function runPlan(plan, { root = ROOT } = {}) {
   282	  if (git(root, 'rev-parse', 'HEAD').trim() !== plan.head) throw new Error('--run requires the selected HEAD checked out');
   283	  if (!plan.worktree && changedPaths(root, plan.head, plan.head, true).length) throw new Error('dirty worktree: use --worktree to bind uncommitted changes');
   284	  const started = new Date().toISOString();
   285	  const receiptDir = join(root, 'target/affected'); mkdirSync(receiptDir, { recursive: true });
   286	  const receiptPath = join(receiptDir, `${plan.head.slice(0, 12)}-${Date.now()}.json`);
   287	  const receipt = { ...plan, started, results: [], complete: false };
   288	  const persist = () => writeFileSync(receiptPath, JSON.stringify(receipt, null, 2) + '\n');
   289	  persist();
   290	  for (const step of plan.steps) {
   291	    console.error(`== ${step.id}\n${formatStep(step)}`);
   292	    const before = Date.now();
   293	    const result = await new Promise(resolveResult => {
   294	      const child = spawn('timeout', ['--kill-after=10s', `${step.timeoutSeconds}s`, ...step.argv], {
   295	        cwd: resolve(root, step.cwd), env: { ...process.env, ...CARGO_ENV }, stdio: 'inherit',
   296	      });
   297	      child.on('error', error => resolveResult({ status: null, error: error.message }));
   298	      child.on('close', (status, signal) => resolveResult({ status, signal }));
   299	    });
   300	    receipt.results.push({ id: step.id, ...result, elapsedMs: Date.now() - before }); persist();
   301	  }
   302	  receipt.complete = true;
   303	  receipt.finished = new Date().toISOString();
   304	  receipt.unchanged = git(root, 'rev-parse', 'HEAD').trim() === plan.head && snapshotDigest(root, plan.observedFiles) === plan.inputDigest
   305	    && JSON.stringify(changedPaths(root, plan.base, plan.head, plan.worktree)) === JSON.stringify(plan.observedFiles);
   306	  receipt.ok = receipt.unchanged && receipt.results.every(result => result.status === 0);
   307	  persist(); console.error(`Receipt: ${receiptPath}; ${receipt.ok ? 'PASS' : 'FAIL'}`);
   308	  return { receiptPath, receipt };
   309	}
   310	async function main() {
   311	  const args = process.argv.slice(2);
   312	  const options = {}; let json = false; let run = false; let proofReceipt;
   313	  for (let i = 0; i < args.length; i++) {
   314	    const arg = args[i];
   315	    if (arg === '--json') json = true;
   316	    else if (arg === '--run') run = true;
   317	    else if (arg === '--complete') options.files = ['snapback4/Cargo.toml'];
   318	    else if (arg === '--worktree') options.worktree = true;
   319	    else if (['--base', '--head', '--proof-receipt'].includes(arg)) {
   320	      if (!args[i + 1] || args[i + 1].startsWith('--')) throw new Error(`${arg} requires a value`);
   321	      if (arg === '--proof-receipt') proofReceipt = args[++i]; else options[arg.slice(2)] = args[++i];
   322	    } else throw new Error(`unknown argument: ${arg}`);
   323	  }
   324	  if (options.worktree && options.head) throw new Error('--head and --worktree are mutually exclusive');
   325	  if (proofReceipt) {
   326	    const receipt = JSON.parse(readFileSync(proofReceipt, 'utf8'));
   327	    options.base = receipt.requestedBaseRevision ?? receipt.headRevision;
   328	    options.head = receipt.headRevision;
   329	    options.worktree = receipt.worktreeIncluded;
   330	    options.files = receipt.cadence === 'complete' ? ['snapback4/Cargo.toml'] : receipt.changedFiles.filter(path => path.startsWith(PREFIX));
   331	  }
   332	  const plan = planAffected(options);
   333	  if (json) console.log(JSON.stringify(plan, null, 2));
   334	  else {
   335	    for (const { path, reason } of plan.reasons) console.log(`# ${path}: ${reason}`);
   336	    for (const step of plan.steps) console.log(formatStep(step));
   337	    console.log(`# ${plan.steps.length} commands, ${plan.rustPackages.length} whole Rust packages, ${plan.clientFiles.length} client files; broad=${plan.broad}`);
   338	  }
   339	  if (run) { const { receipt } = await runPlan(plan); if (!receipt.ok) process.exitCode = 1; }
   340	}

## Author ruling (recorded 2026-08-11, Charlie Cheever in session)

All three points ruled as specified: (1) the 2026-08-10 24-hour landing-author
priority is preserved, after which an unclaimed or inactive ticket becomes
fix-forward-by-anyone; (2) the narrow reversal of LLP 0175 D2.1's
“infrastructure ambiguity still blocks” ruling is approved only when the same
runner-controlled failure is corroborated at every merge-base — effective when
the D-3 implementation lands, with fail-closed behavior standing until then;
(3) a later, separately reviewed automated-revert activation decision may
proceed under D-7 after pilot evidence. That last ruling activates nothing here. Admitting nondeterministic outcomes non-blocking would be a separate future carve-out requiring its own ruling and amendment to LLP 0175; this RFC does not request it. Urgent security exposure, ongoing destructive data corruption, or complete publication inability triggers immediate author escalation, never autonomous action under this RFC.

## Optimistic landing default (2026-08-13)

Author ruling by Charlie Cheever, recorded in session as a living amendment to
this accepted RFC: **ordinary source landings optimize for throughput and
fix-forward recovery.** When the affected tests passed on the candidate, the
tested semantic delta survives integration unchanged, and merge/rebase adds no
semantic conflict resolution, the candidate lands immediately. Advancement of
`main` alone does not require repeating expensive verification. If integration
changes a tested semantic input, the checks whose inputs changed run again;
known failing affected product tests still require attention before landing.
This creates no exact-HEAD retest, merge queue, serializer, quiet-main window,
or new receipt ceremony.

The tracked pre-push hook still consumes every ref update and runs the checked
LLP 0175 selector. For a revision targeting `origin/main`, ordinary mode
reports the selected frozen-record gates and defers their commands to the
two-hour `drift-prone-gates.mjs backstop`.
The backstop remains authoritative for frozen-record health and converts reds
into visible, attributable, idempotent fix-forward tickets. The complete
canonical battery remains authoritative under LLP 0189 D-1. Selection is
retained because it gives immediate scope visibility and keeps the explicit
strict path honest; a selected gate is not an ordinary landing veto.

A ref update not targeting `origin/main` is not called scheduled or deferred:
ordinary mode reports its selected gates, runs none, and states that no
scheduled-main verdict is claimed. If that revision later lands on
`origin/main`, that landing's own ref update is selected and becomes backstop
input. A same-tip main/non-main push reports both ref dispositions while the
one revision is covered once by the main landing. Explicit strict mode
continues to execute every selected revision, including multi-ref and non-main
pushes.

`SNAPBACK_DRIFT_GATES_STRICT=1 git push ...` explicitly restores synchronous
execution and D-3's complete outcome protocol: introduced content reds and
nondeterminism block; inherited reds and corroborated runner infrastructure
proceed with their existing attribution and ticket requests. Add
`SNAPBACK_DRIFT_GATES_FULL=1` to select the complete registry. For compatibility,
`SNAPBACK_DRIFT_GATES_FULL=1` by itself implies strict full-registry execution,
and `SNAPBACK_DRIFT_GATES_BYPASS=1` remains accepted and loud even though its

   250	export function changedPaths(root, base, head, worktree = false) {
   251	  const committed = paths(git(root, 'diff', '--name-only', '-z', '--no-renames', `${base}...${head}`));
   252	  return sorted([...committed, ...(worktree ? [
   253	    ...paths(git(root, 'diff', '--name-only', '-z', '--no-renames', 'HEAD')),
   254	    ...paths(git(root, 'ls-files', '--others', '--exclude-standard', '-z')),
   255	  ] : [])]);
   256	}
   257	function snapshotDigest(root, files) {
   258	  return hash(JSON.stringify(files.map(path => [path, existsSync(join(root, path)) ? hash(readFileSync(join(root, path))) : null])));
   259	}

   250	export function changedPaths(root, base, head, worktree = false) {
   283	  const plan = worktree ? select(loadModel(root)) : modelAtRevision(root, headRevision, select);
   284	  return { ...plan, base: baseRevision, mergeBase: git(root, 'merge-base', baseRevision, headRevision).trim(), head: headRevision,
   285	    worktree, observedFiles, inputDigest: snapshotDigest(root, observedFiles) };

   287	const quote = value => `'${value.replaceAll("'", "'\\''")}'`;
   288	export function formatStep(step) {
   289	  return `(cd ${quote(step.cwd)} && timeout ${step.timeoutSeconds}s env ${Object.entries(CARGO_ENV).map(([key, value]) => `${key}=${value}`).join(' ')} ${step.argv.map(quote).join(' ')})`;
   290	}
   291	export async function runPlan(plan, { root = ROOT } = {}) {
   292	  if (git(root, 'rev-parse', 'HEAD').trim() !== plan.head) throw new Error('--run requires the selected HEAD checked out');
   293	  if (!plan.worktree && changedPaths(root, plan.head, plan.head, true).length) throw new Error('dirty worktree: use --worktree to bind uncommitted changes');
   294	  const started = new Date().toISOString();
   295	  const receiptDir = join(root, 'target/affected'); mkdirSync(receiptDir, { recursive: true });
   296	  const receiptPath = join(receiptDir, `${plan.head.slice(0, 12)}-${Date.now()}.json`);
   297	  const receipt = { ...plan, started, results: [], complete: false };
   298	  const persist = () => writeFileSync(receiptPath, JSON.stringify(receipt, null, 2) + '\n');
   299	  persist();
   300	  for (const step of plan.steps) {
   301	    console.error(`== ${step.id}\n${formatStep(step)}`);
   302	    const before = Date.now();
   303	    const result = await new Promise(resolveResult => {
   304	      const child = spawn('timeout', ['--kill-after=10s', `${step.timeoutSeconds}s`, ...step.argv], {
   305	        cwd: resolve(root, step.cwd), env: { ...process.env, ...CARGO_ENV }, stdio: 'inherit',
   306	      });
   307	      child.on('error', error => resolveResult({ status: null, error: error.message }));
   308	      child.on('close', (status, signal) => resolveResult({ status, signal }));
   309	    });
   310	    receipt.results.push({ id: step.id, ...result, elapsedMs: Date.now() - before }); persist();
   311	  }
   312	  receipt.complete = true;
   313	  receipt.finished = new Date().toISOString();
   314	  receipt.unchanged = git(root, 'rev-parse', 'HEAD').trim() === plan.head && snapshotDigest(root, plan.observedFiles) === plan.inputDigest
   315	    && JSON.stringify(changedPaths(root, plan.base, plan.head, plan.worktree)) === JSON.stringify(plan.observedFiles);
   316	  receipt.ok = receipt.unchanged && receipt.results.every(result => result.status === 0);
   317	  persist(); console.error(`Receipt: ${receiptPath}; ${receipt.ok ? 'PASS' : 'FAIL'}`);
   318	  return { receiptPath, receipt };
   319	}
   320	async function main() {
   321	  const args = process.argv.slice(2);
   322	  const options = {}; let json = false; let run = false; let proofReceipt;
   323	  for (let i = 0; i < args.length; i++) {
   324	    const arg = args[i];
   325	    if (arg === '--json') json = true;
   326	    else if (arg === '--run') run = true;
   327	    else if (arg === '--complete') options.files = ['snapback4/Cargo.toml'];
   328	    else if (arg === '--worktree') options.worktree = true;
   329	    else if (['--base', '--head', '--proof-receipt'].includes(arg)) {
   330	      if (!args[i + 1] || args[i + 1].startsWith('--')) throw new Error(`${arg} requires a value`);
   331	      if (arg === '--proof-receipt') proofReceipt = args[++i]; else options[arg.slice(2)] = args[++i];
   332	    } else throw new Error(`unknown argument: ${arg}`);
   333	  }
   334	  if (options.worktree && options.head) throw new Error('--head and --worktree are mutually exclusive');
   335	  if (proofReceipt) {
   336	    const receipt = JSON.parse(readFileSync(proofReceipt, 'utf8'));
   337	    options.base = receipt.requestedBaseRevision ?? receipt.headRevision;
   338	    options.head = receipt.headRevision;
   339	    options.worktree = receipt.worktreeIncluded;
   340	    options.files = receipt.cadence === 'complete' ? ['snapback4/Cargo.toml'] : receipt.changedFiles.filter(path => path.startsWith(PREFIX));

    28	fn control_socket(deployment: &Path) -> PathBuf {
    29	    use std::os::unix::ffi::OsStrExt;
    30	    use sha2::{Digest, Sha256};
    31	    let canonical = deployment.canonicalize().unwrap();
    32	    let hash = Sha256::digest(canonical.as_os_str().as_bytes());
    33	    PathBuf::from(format!("/tmp/snapback4-{}/{}.sock", unsafe { libc::geteuid() },
    34	        hash.iter().map(|b| format!("{b:02x}")).collect::<String>()))
    35	}

     1	//! @ref LLP 3000.009.001.000#32-the-rule — no database connection above the port.
     2	//!
     3	//! Counts matching *source lines* once per file and category, not substrings or
     4	//! individual calls. A small lexer removes comments and literal contents (including
     5	//! raw strings), retains line numbers, and matches whole Rust tokens across lines.
     6	//! Patterns: rusqlite::, postgres::, .conn(), .query_row(, .query_map(,
     7	//! .prepare(, .prepare_cached(, .execute(, .execute_batch(,
     8	//! unchecked_transaction, Transaction::new_unchecked, Connection::open.
     9	//! Raw Store/Tx device, installation and fixture methods count too, including
    10	//! UFCS calls and method references. Device calls belong only to snapback4-device
    11	//! and the adapters; renaming generic metadata access does not evade the port.
    12	//! Explicit device methods count as production even inside consumer test modules;
    13	//! those modules use the debug-only fixture hooks when raw access is needed.
    14	//! The DeviceStore/DeviceTx trait spellings (meta, set_meta, etc.) count as well.
    15	//! Transaction and Connection require exact type identifiers, excluding
    16	//! AuthorityTransaction and StepTransaction. Connection::open also includes
    17	//! open_* constructors (e.g. open_with_flags); literals exclude HTTP headers. The four generic
    18	//! prepare/execute methods require an argument: database APIs need one, whereas
    19	//! Records::prepare / records.prepare() and witness Run::execute() do not.
    20	//!
    21	//! This is a lexical ratchet, not type checking: macro-generated calls,
    22	//! and database APIs outside these spellings can escape it; a non-database method
    23	//! with the same name and arguments can count. cfg(test) *inline modules* are
    24	//! separated by token brace depth (braces in comments/literals do not interfere).
    25	//! Other cfg expressions/functions and external module declarations are not
    26	//! interpreted. Rust files under tests/, with "tests" in their filename, or
    27	//! named test_cases.rs are wholly test code. Driver patterns scan the core and host src trees; raw
    28	//! Store/Tx methods scan every workspace crate's src tree except snapback4-device.
    29	//! Adapter exclusions apply to both categories. A mixed file can appear in both allowlists.
    30	//! Crate renames through use/extern crate are followed within each source file.
    31	//! Cross-file alias re-exports and arbitrary type/macro resolution remain limits.
    32	//!
    33	//! From snapback4/: SNAPBACK4_RATCHET_UPDATE=1 cargo test -p snapback4
    34	//! --test store_port_ratchet. A count below its listed number only prints a note
    35	//! (never fails), so removing access stays green. Updates only lower/delete counts by default;
    36	//! SNAPBACK4_RATCHET_ALLOW_RAISE=1 is additionally required for any increase or
    37	//! new entry (including initial generation). Review the resulting data diff.
    38	
    39	use std::collections::{BTreeMap, BTreeSet};
    40	use std::fs;
    41	use std::path::{Path, PathBuf};
    42	
    43	type Counts = BTreeMap<String, usize>;
    44	const RULE: &str = "LLP 3000.009.001.000 §3.2";

   300	export function formatStep(step) {
   301	  return `(cd ${quote(step.cwd)} && timeout ${step.timeoutSeconds}s env RUST_MIN_STACK=33554432 ${Object.entries(CARGO_ENV).map(([key, value]) => `${key}=${value}`).join(' ')} ${step.argv.map(quote).join(' ')})`;

   303	export async function runPlan(plan, { root = ROOT } = {}) {
   304	  if (git(root, 'rev-parse', 'HEAD').trim() !== plan.head) throw new Error('--run requires the selected HEAD checked out');
   305	  if (!plan.worktree && changedPaths(root, plan.head, plan.head, true).length) throw new Error('dirty worktree: use --worktree to bind uncommitted changes');
   306	  const started = new Date().toISOString();
   307	  const receiptDir = join(root, 'target/affected'); mkdirSync(receiptDir, { recursive: true });
   308	  const receiptPath = join(receiptDir, `${plan.head.slice(0, 12)}-${Date.now()}.json`);
   309	  const receipt = { ...plan, started, results: [], complete: false };
   310	  const persist = () => writeFileSync(receiptPath, JSON.stringify(receipt, null, 2) + '\n');
   311	  persist();
   312	  for (const step of plan.steps) {
   313	    console.error(`== ${step.id}\n${formatStep(step)}`);
   314	    const before = Date.now();
   315	    const result = await new Promise(resolveResult => {
   316	      const child = spawn('timeout', ['--kill-after=10s', `${step.timeoutSeconds}s`, ...step.argv], {
   317	        cwd: resolve(root, step.cwd), env: { ...process.env, ...CARGO_ENV, RUST_MIN_STACK: '33554432' }, stdio: 'inherit',
   318	      });
   319	      child.on('error', error => resolveResult({ status: null, error: error.message }));
   320	      child.on('close', (status, signal) => resolveResult({ status, signal }));
   321	    });
   322	    receipt.results.push({ id: step.id, ...result, elapsedMs: Date.now() - before }); persist();
   323	  }
   324	  receipt.complete = true;
   325	  receipt.finished = new Date().toISOString();
   326	  receipt.unchanged = git(root, 'rev-parse', 'HEAD').trim() === plan.head && snapshotDigest(root, plan.observedFiles) === plan.inputDigest
   327	    && JSON.stringify(changedPaths(root, plan.base, plan.head, plan.worktree)) === JSON.stringify(plan.observedFiles);
   328	  receipt.ok = receipt.unchanged && receipt.results.every(result => result.status === 0);
   329	  persist(); console.error(`Receipt: ${receiptPath}; ${receipt.ok ? 'PASS' : 'FAIL'}`);
   330	  return { receiptPath, receipt };
   331	}
   332	async function main() {
   333	  const args = process.argv.slice(2);
   334	  const options = {}; let json = false; let run = false; let proofReceipt;
   335	  for (let i = 0; i < args.length; i++) {
   336	    const arg = args[i];
   337	    if (arg === '--json') json = true;
   338	    else if (arg === '--run') run = true;
   339	    else if (arg === '--complete') options.files = ['snapback4/Cargo.toml'];
   340	    else if (arg === '--worktree') options.worktree = true;
   341	    else if (['--base', '--head', '--proof-receipt'].includes(arg)) {
   342	      if (!args[i + 1] || args[i + 1].startsWith('--')) throw new Error(`${arg} requires a value`);
   343	      if (arg === '--proof-receipt') proofReceipt = args[++i]; else options[arg.slice(2)] = args[++i];
   344	    } else throw new Error(`unknown argument: ${arg}`);
   345	  }
   346	  if (options.worktree && options.head) throw new Error('--head and --worktree are mutually exclusive');
   347	  if (proofReceipt) {
   348	    const receipt = JSON.parse(readFileSync(proofReceipt, 'utf8'));
   349	    options.base = receipt.requestedBaseRevision ?? receipt.headRevision;
   350	    options.head = receipt.headRevision;
   351	    options.worktree = receipt.worktreeIncluded;
   352	    options.files = receipt.cadence === 'complete' ? ['snapback4/Cargo.toml'] : receipt.changedFiles.filter(path => path.startsWith(PREFIX));
   353	  }
   354	  const plan = planAffected(options);
   355	  if (json) console.log(JSON.stringify(plan, null, 2));
   356	  else {
   357	    for (const { path, reason } of plan.reasons) console.log(`# ${path}: ${reason}`);
   358	    for (const step of plan.steps) console.log(formatStep(step));
   359	    console.log(`# ${plan.steps.length} commands, ${plan.rustPackages.length} whole Rust packages, ${plan.clientFiles.length} client files; broad=${plan.broad}`);
   360	  }
   361	  if (run) { const { receipt } = await runPlan(plan); if (!receipt.ok) process.exitCode = 1; }
   362	}

NOT READY

- **P1 — Root fail-closed delegation becomes empty.** [affected.mjs:352](/home/ccheever/projects/sb4-fast/snapback4/scripts/affected.mjs:352) discards every non-`snapback4/` path without honoring the root receipt’s `FAIL_CLOSED_FULL` classification. A root `rust-toolchain.toml` or unknown-path change selects the delegation gate, but its delegated plan contains **zero commands**. The test at [affected.test.mjs:71](/home/ccheever/projects/sb4-fast/snapback4/scripts/affected.test.mjs:71) checks classification, not delegated execution.

- **P1 — Broad selection omits existing checks.** The command construction at [affected.mjs:222](/home/ccheever/projects/sb4-fast/snapback4/scripts/affected.mjs:222) never selects fixture-wrapper tests, chat schema checking, chat/Expo typechecks, or release guards—all present in [test.sh:128](/home/ccheever/projects/sb4-fast/snapback4/scripts/test.sh:128). Even changing `fixtures/clique.mjs` omits its own `clique.test.mjs`. `--complete` uses this same incomplete broad plan. This undermines the amendment’s fail-broad promise and D-1’s complete scheduled battery.

- **P1 — Bare workspace imports do not trigger client rebuilding.** [affected.mjs:64](/home/ccheever/projects/sb4-fast/snapback4/scripts/affected.mjs:64) recognizes client-build consumers through literal `/dist/` text; resolving `snapback4/auth` to a dist export does not add that evidence. Consequently, a test-only change to `headless-registration.test.mjs` selects CLI/wasm builds but omits `build-client`, despite its package imports at [headless-registration.test.mjs:9](/home/ccheever/projects/sb4-fast/snapback4/packages/snapback4/test/headless-registration.test.mjs:9). Execution can fail on a fresh checkout or test stale ignored dist files.

- **P1 — Documentation narrowing misses executable consumers.** [affected.mjs:139](/home/ccheever/projects/sb4-fast/snapback4/scripts/affected.mjs:139) treats markdown as docs-only. Changing `SERVER.md` selects neither the compiled guide suite nor `readme-examples.test.mjs`. Changing `guide/client.md` selects the Rust guide target but still omits the TypeScript snippet checks that consume it at [readme-examples.test.mjs:99](/home/ccheever/projects/sb4-fast/snapback4/packages/snapback4/test/readme-examples.test.mjs:99).

- **P2 — Committed-mode receipts can falsely report unchanged inputs.** At [affected.mjs:326](/home/ccheever/projects/sb4-fast/snapback4/scripts/affected.mjs:326), only originally changed files are hashed; the closing path comparison excludes worktree changes when `plan.worktree` is false. Modifying another tracked dependency or test during execution can therefore leave `unchanged: true` and `ok: true`. This contradicts the amendment’s snapshot guarantee. The executor test covers keep-going behavior, not this mutation case.

Read-only selection probes confirmed the selection examples. No files were modified by this review. Historical execution and full timing remain unestablished; fixture replay does not establish either.
